\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mathsf{b}}\log\frac{N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{N(s,a)}}\frac{\widehat{P}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)V(s^{\prime})-\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}V\big)\widehat{P}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)}{\sqrt{\widehat{P}_{s,a}(V\circ V)-\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}V\big)^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mathsf{b}}\log\frac{N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{N(s,a)}}\frac{\widehat{P}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)V(s^{\prime})}{\sqrt{\widehat{P}_{s,a}(V\circ V)-\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}V\big)^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma)\widehat{P}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)V(s^{\prime})\leq\gamma\widehat{P}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a),

where the penultimate inequality relies on (
141
), and the last inequality is valid since

V(s^{\prime})=\max_{a}Q(s^{\prime},a)\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
and
\gamma\geq 1/2
.
In turn, the preceding relation allows one to derive

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}(Q)(s,a)\big)}{\partial V(s^{\prime})}=\gamma\widehat{P}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)-\frac{\partial b(s,a;V)}{\partial V(s^{\prime})}\geq 0

for any
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
.

Putting the above cases together reveals that

\frac{\partial\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}(Q)(s,a)\big)}{\partial V(s^{\prime})}\geq 0\qquad\text{for all }(s,a,s^{\prime})\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{S}

holds almost everywhere (except for the boundary points of these cases).
Recognizing that
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}(Q)
is continuous in
Q
and that
V
is non-decreasing in
Q
,
one can immediately conclude that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}({Q})\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}(\widetilde{Q})\qquad\text{for any }Q\leq\widetilde{Q}.

(142)

Existence and uniqueness of fixed points.

To begin with, note that
for any
0\leq Q\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\cdot 1
, one has
0\leq\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}(Q)\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\cdot 1
.
If we produce the following sequence recursively:

Q^{(0)}=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad Q^{(t+1)}=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}(Q^{(t)})\quad\text{for all }t\geq 0,

then the standard proof for the Banach fixed-point theorem (e.g.,
Agarwal et al., 2001
) tells us that
Q^{(t)}
converges to some point
Q^{(\infty)}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. Clearly,
Q^{(\infty)}
is a fixed point of
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}
obeying
0\leq Q^{(\infty)}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\cdot 1
.

We then turn to justifying the uniqueness of fixed points of
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}
.
Suppose that there exists another point
\widetilde{Q}
obeying
\widetilde{Q}=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}\big(\widetilde{Q}\big)
,
which clearly satisfies
\widetilde{Q}\geq 0
.
If
\|\widetilde{Q}\|_{\infty}>\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
, then

\big\|\widetilde{Q}\big\|_{\infty}=\big\|\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}\big(\widetilde{Q}\big)\big\|_{\infty}\leq\|r\|_{\infty}+\gamma\|\widehat{P}\|_{1}\big\|\widetilde{Q}\big\|_{\infty}\leq 1+\gamma\big\|\widetilde{Q}\big\|_{\infty}<(1-\gamma)\big\|\widetilde{Q}\big\|_{\infty}+\gamma\big\|\widetilde{Q}\big\|_{\infty}=\big\|\widetilde{Q}\big\|_{\infty},

resulting in contradiction. Consequently, one necessarily has
0\leq\widetilde{Q}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\cdot 1
.
Further, the
\gamma
-contraction property (
139
) implies that

\big\|\widetilde{Q}-Q^{(\infty)}\big\|_{\infty}=\big\|\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}\big(\widetilde{Q}\big)-\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}\big(Q^{(\infty)}\big)\big\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma\big\|\widetilde{Q}-Q^{(\infty)}\big\|_{\infty}.

Given that
\gamma<1
, this inequality cannot happen unless
\widetilde{Q}=Q^{{\infty}}
,
thus confirming the uniqueness of
Q^{{\infty}}
.

A.2
Proof of Lemma
2

Let us first recall the monotone non-decreasing property (
137
) of the operator
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}
defined in (
135
), which taken together with the property (
136
) readily yields

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}(Q)\leq\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}(\widetilde{Q})

(143)

for any
Q
and
\widetilde{Q}
obeying
Q\leq\widetilde{Q}
,
0\leq Q\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\cdot 1
and
0\leq\widetilde{Q}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\cdot 1
(with
1
the all-one vector).
Given that
\widehat{Q}_{0}=0\leq\widehat{Q}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}
, we can apply (
143
) to obtain

\widehat{Q}_{1}=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}\big(Q_{0}\big)\leq\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}\big(\widehat{Q}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big)=\widehat{Q}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}.

Repeat this argument recursively to arrive at

\widehat{Q}_{\tau}\leq\widehat{Q}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\qquad\text{for all }\tau\geq 0.

In addition, it comes directly from Lemma
1
that

\displaystyle\big\|\widehat{Q}_{\tau}-\widehat{Q}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\big\|\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}\big(\widehat{Q}_{\tau-1}\big)-\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}\big(\widehat{Q}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big)\big\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma\big\|\widehat{Q}_{\tau-1}-\widehat{Q}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\cdots\leq\gamma^{\tau}\big\|\widehat{Q}_{0}-\widehat{Q}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma^{\tau}}{1-\gamma}

(144)

for any
\tau\geq 0
,
where the last inequality is valid since
\widehat{Q}_{0}=0
and
\|\widehat{Q}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
(see Lemma
1
).
The other claim (
33
) also follows immediately by taking the right-hand side of (
144
) to be no larger than
1/N
.

A.3
Proof of Lemma
4

For any
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
, if
\frac{Nd^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)}{12}<\frac{2}{3}\log\frac{SN}{\delta}
,
then it is self-evident that this pair satisfies (
69
).
As a consequence, it suffices to focus attention on the following set of state-action pairs:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathsf{large}}\coloneqq\bigg\{(s,a)\,\Big|\,d^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)\geq\frac{8\log\frac{SN}{\delta}}{N}\bigg\}.

(145)

To bound the cardinality of
\mathcal{N}_{\mathsf{large}}
, we make the observation that

\big|\mathcal{N}_{\mathsf{large}}\big|\cdot\frac{8\log\frac{SN}{\delta}}{N}\leq\sum_{(s,a)\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathsf{large}}}d^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)\leq\sum_{(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}}d^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)\leq 1,

thus leading to the crude bound

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{N}_{\mathsf{large}}\big|\leq\frac{N}{8\log\frac{SN}{\delta}}\leq\frac{N}{8}.

(146)

Let us now look at any
(s,a)\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathsf{large}}
.
Given that
N(s,a)
can be viewed as the sum of
N
independent Bernoulli random variables each with mean
d^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)
,
we can apply the Bernstein inequality to yield

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big\{\left|N(s,a)-Nd^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)\right|\geq\tau\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{\tau^{2}/2}{v_{s,a}+\tau/3}\right)

for any
\tau\geq 0
,
where we define

v_{s,a}\coloneqq N\mathsf{Var}\Big(\mathds{1}\big\{(s_{i},a_{i})=(s,a)\big\}\Big)\leq Nd^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a).

A little algebra then yields that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\left|N(s,a)-Nd^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{4v_{s,a}\log\frac{2}{\delta}}+\frac{2}{3}\log\frac{2}{\delta}\leq\sqrt{4Nd^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)\log\frac{2}{\delta}}+\log\frac{2}{\delta}.

(147)

Combining this result with the union bound over
(s,a)\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathsf{large}}
and making use of (
146
) give: with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\left|N(s,a)-Nd^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)\right|\leq\sqrt{4Nd^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)\log\frac{N}{\delta}}+\log\frac{N}{\delta}

(148)

holds simultaneously for all
(s,a)\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathsf{large}}
.
Recalling that
Nd^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)\geq 8\log\frac{NS}{\delta}
holds for any
(s,a)\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathsf{large}}
,
we can easily verify that

\displaystyle N(s,a)\geq Nd^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)-\left(\sqrt{4Nd^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)\log\frac{N}{\delta}}+\log\frac{N}{\delta}\right)\geq\frac{Nd^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)}{12},

(149)

thereby establishing (
69
) for any
(s,a)\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathsf{large}}
. This concludes the proof.

A.4
Proof of Lemma
5

If
N(s,a)=0
, then the inequalities hold trivially. Hence, it is sufficient to focus on the case where
N(s,a)>0
.
Before proceeding, we make note of a key Bernstein-style result; the proof is deferred to Appendix
A.4.1
.

Lemma 9
.

Consider any given pair
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
with
N(s,a)>0
. Let
V\in\mathbb{R}^{S}
be any vector independent of
\widehat{P}_{s,a}
obeying
\|V\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
.
With probability at least
1-4\delta
, one has

\displaystyle\big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)V\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{48\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}(V)\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{N(s,a)}}+\frac{48\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)}

(150a)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}(V)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(V\big)+\frac{5\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}

(150b)

Remark
6
.

In words, Lemma
9
develops a Bernstein bound (
150a
) on
\big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)V\big|

that makes clear the importance of the variance parameter. Lemma
9
(cf. (
150b
)) also ascertains that the variance w.r.t. the empirical distribution
\widehat{P}_{s,a}
does not deviate much from the variance w.r.t. the true distribution
P_{s,a}
.

Equipped with this result, we are now ready to present the proof of Lemma
5
, which is built upon a leave-one-out decoupling argument and consists of the following steps.

Step 1: construction of auxiliary state-absorbing MDPs.

Recall that
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
is the empirical MDP.
For each state
s\in\mathcal{S}
and each scalar
u\geq 0
, we construct an auxiliary state-absorbing MDP
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{s,u}
in a way that makes it identical to the empirical MDP
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
except for state
s
.
More specifically, the transition kernel of the auxiliary MDP
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{s,u}
— denoted by
P^{s,u}
— is chosen such that

\displaystyle P^{s,u}(\,\widetilde{s}\,|\,s,a)

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}(\widetilde{s}=s)\qquad\quad

\displaystyle\text{for all }(\,\widetilde{s},a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A},

\displaystyle P^{s,u}(\cdot\,|\,s^{\prime},a)

\displaystyle=\widehat{P}(\cdot\,|\,s^{\prime},a)\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for all }(s^{\prime},a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\text{ and }s^{\prime}\neq s;

and the reward function of
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{s,u}
— denoted by
r^{s,u}
— is set to be

\displaystyle r^{s,u}(s,a)

\displaystyle=u\qquad\qquad\quad~

\displaystyle\text{for all }a\in\mathcal{A},

\displaystyle r^{s,u}(s^{\prime},a)

\displaystyle=r(s^{\prime},a)\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for all }(s^{\prime},a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\text{ and }s^{\prime}\neq s.

In words, the probability transition kernel of
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{s,u}
is obtained by dropping all randomness of
\widehat{P}_{s,a}
(
a\in\mathcal{A}
) that concerns state
s
and making
s
an absorbing state.
In addition, let us define the pessimistic Bellman operator
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{s,u}
based on the auxiliary MDP
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{s,u}
such that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{s,u}(Q)(s,a)\coloneqq\max\Big\{r^{s,u}(s,a)+\gamma P^{s,u}_{s,a}V-b^{s,u}(s,a;V),\,0\Big\}

(151)

for any
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
,
where the penalty term is taken to be

\displaystyle b^{s,u}(s,a;V)=\min\Bigg\{\max\bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mathsf{b}}\log\frac{N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{N(s,a)}\mathsf{Var}_{P^{s,u}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)}(V)},\,\frac{2c_{\mathsf{b}}\log\frac{N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)}\bigg\},\,\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\Bigg\}+\frac{5}{N}.

(152)

Step 2: the correspondence between the empirical MDP and auxiliary MDP.

Taking

\displaystyle u^{\star}=(1-\gamma)\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}(s)+\min\left\{\frac{2c_{\mathsf{b}}\log\frac{N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)\max_{a}N(s,a)},\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\right\}+\frac{5}{N},

(153)

we claim that there exists a fixed point
\widehat{Q}^{\star}_{s,u^{\star}}
of
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{s,u^{\star}}
whose corresponding value function
\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}
coincides with
\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}
.
To justify this, it suffices to verify the following properties:

•

Consider any
a\in\mathcal{A}
. Given that
P^{s,u}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)
only has a single non-zero entry (equal to 1), it is easily seen that

\mathsf{Var}_{P^{s,u}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)}(V)=0
holds for any
V
and any
u
, thus indicating that

\displaystyle b^{s,u}(s,a;V)=\min\Bigg\{\frac{2c_{\mathsf{b}}\log\frac{N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)},\,\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\Bigg\}+\frac{5}{N}.

(154)

Consequently, for state
s
, one has

\displaystyle\max_{a}\Big\{r^{s,u^{\star}}(s,a)-b^{s,u^{\star}}\big(s,a;\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big)+\gamma\big\langle P^{s,u^{\star}}(\cdot\,|\,s,a),\,\,\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big\rangle\Big\}

\displaystyle=\max_{a}\Big\{u^{\star}-b^{s,u^{\star}}\big(s,a;\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big)+\gamma\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}(s)\Big\}

\displaystyle=u^{\star}-\min_{a}b^{s,u^{\star}}\big(s,a;\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big)+\gamma\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}(s)

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma)\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}(s)+\gamma\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}(s)

\displaystyle=\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}(s),

(155)

where the third identity makes use of our choice (
153
) of
u^{\star}
and (
154
).

•

Next, consider any
s^{\prime}\neq s
and any
a\in\mathcal{A}
. We make the observation that

\displaystyle\max\Big\{r^{s,u^{\star}}(s^{\prime},a)-b^{s,u^{\star}}\big(s^{\prime},a;\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big)+\gamma\big\langle P^{s,u^{\star}}(\cdot\,|\,s^{\prime},a),\,\,\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big\rangle,\,0\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\max\Big\{r(s^{\prime},a)-b\big(s^{\prime},a;\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big)+\gamma\big\langle\widehat{P}(\cdot\,|\,s^{\prime},a),\,\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big\rangle,\,0\Big\}=\widehat{Q}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}(s^{\prime},a),

(156)

where the last relation holds since
\widehat{Q}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}
is a fixed point of
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}
.

Armed with (
155
) and (
156
),
we see that
\widehat{Q}^{\star}_{s,u^{\star}}=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{s,u^{\star}}\big(\widehat{Q}^{\star}_{s,u^{\star}}\big)
by taking

\displaystyle\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\widehat{Q}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}(s,a)

\displaystyle=\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}(s),\qquad

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}(s^{\prime},a)

\displaystyle=\widehat{Q}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}(s^{\prime},a)\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for all }s^{\prime}\neq s\text{ and }a\in\mathcal{A}.

This readily confirms the existence of a fixed point of
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{s,u^{\star}}
whose corresponding value coincides with
\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}
.

Step 3: building an
\epsilon
-net.

Consider any
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
with
N(s,a)>0
. Construct a set
\mathcal{U}_{\text{cover}}
as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\text{cover}}\coloneqq\left\{\frac{i}{N}\mid 1\leq i\leq Nu_{\max}\right\},

(157)

with
u_{\max}=\min\Big\{\frac{2c_{\mathsf{b}}\log\frac{N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)},\,\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\Big\}+\frac{5}{N}+1
.
This can be viewed as the
\epsilon
-net
(
Vershynin, 2018
)
of the range
[0,u_{\max}]\subseteq\big[0,\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\big]
with
\epsilon=1/N
.
Let us construct an auxiliary MDP
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{s,u}
as in Step 1 for each
u\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{cover}}
.
Repeating the argument in the proof of Lemma
1
(see Section
A.1
),
we can easily show that there exists a unique fixed point
\widehat{Q}^{\star}_{s,u}
of
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{s,u}
,
which also obeys

0\leq\widehat{Q}^{\star}_{s,u}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\cdot 1
.
In what follows, we denote by
\widehat{V}^{\star}_{s,u}
the corresponding value function of
\widehat{Q}^{\star}_{s,u}
.

Recognizing that
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{s,u}
is statistically independent from
\widehat{P}_{s,a}
for any
u\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{cover}}
(by construction),
we can apply Lemma
9
in conjunction with the union bound (over all
u\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{cover}}
) to show that,
with probability exceeding
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\Big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)\widehat{V}_{s,u}^{\star}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{48\log\frac{8N^{2}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{N(s,a)}\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u}^{\star}\big)}+\frac{48\log\frac{8N^{2}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)},

(158a)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u}^{\star}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u}^{\star}\big)+\frac{5\log\frac{8N^{2}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}

(158b)

hold simultaneously for all
u\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{cover}}
.
Clearly, the total number of
(s,a)
pairs with
N(s,a)>0
cannot exceed
N
. Thus, taking the union bound over all these pairs yield that,
with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\Big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)\widehat{V}_{s,u}^{\star}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{48\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{N(s,a)}\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u}^{\star}\big)}+\frac{48\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)},

(159a)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u}^{\star}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u}^{\star}\big)+\frac{5\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}

(159b)

hold simultaneously for all
(s,a,u)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{U}_{\text{cover}}
obeying
N(s,a)>0
.

Step 4: a covering argument.

In this step, we shall work on the high-probability event (
158
) that holds simultaneously for all
u\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{cover}}
.
Given that
\widehat{V}^{\star}_{\mathsf{pe}}
satisfies the trivial bound
0\leq\widehat{V}^{\star}_{\mathsf{pe}}(s)\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
,
one can find some
u_{0}\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{cover}}
such that
|u_{0}-u^{\star}|\leq 1/N
,
where we recall the choice of
u^{\star}
in (
153
).
From the definition of the MDP
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{s,u}
and the operator (
151
),
it is readily seen that

\big\|\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{s,u_{0}}(Q)-\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{s,u^{\star}}(Q)\big\|_{\infty}\leq\big|u_{0}-u^{\star}\big|\leq\frac{1}{N}

holds for any
Q\in\mathbb{R}^{SA}
. Consequently, we can use
\gamma
-contraction of the operator to obtain

\displaystyle\big\|\widehat{Q}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}-\widehat{Q}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{s,u_{0}}\big(\widehat{Q}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)-\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{s,u^{\star}}\big(\widehat{Q}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)\Big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{s,u^{\star}}\big(\widehat{Q}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)-\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{s,u^{\star}}\big(\widehat{Q}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)\Big\|_{\infty}+\Big\|\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{s,u_{0}}\big(\widehat{Q}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)-\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{s,u^{\star}}\big(\widehat{Q}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)\Big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\big\|\widehat{Q}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}-\widehat{Q}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}+\frac{1}{N},

which implies that

\big\|\widehat{Q}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}-\widehat{Q}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)N}

and therefore

\big\|\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}-\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}\leq\big\|\widehat{Q}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}-\widehat{Q}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)N}.

This in turn allows us to demonstrate that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)-\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad=P_{s,a}\Big(\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}-P_{s,a}\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)\circ\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}-P_{s,a}\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)-\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}-P_{s,a}\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)\circ\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}-P_{s,a}\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{s,a}\Big(\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}-P_{s,a}\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)\circ\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}-P_{s,a}\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)-\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}-P_{s,a}\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)\circ\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}-P_{s,a}\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{s,a}\Big(\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}-P_{s,a}\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}+\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}-P_{s,a}\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)\circ\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}-\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\Big|P_{s,a}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}-\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)\Big|\leq\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\big\|\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}-\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N},

where the third line comes from the fact that
\mathbb{E}[X]=\arg\min_{c}\mathbb{E}[(X-c)^{2}]
, and the last line relies on the property
0\leq\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star},\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
.
In addition, by swapping
\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}
and
\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}
, we can derive

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)-\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)\leq\frac{2}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N},

and then

\displaystyle\Big|\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)-\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)\Big|\leq\frac{2}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N}.

(160)

Clearly, this bound (
160
) continues to be valid if we replace
P_{s,a}
with
\widehat{P}_{s,a}
.

With the above perturbation bounds in mind, we can invoke the triangle inequality and (
159a
) to reach

\displaystyle\Big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\Big|\leq\Big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\Big|+\Big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}-\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\Big|+\frac{2}{N(1-\gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{48\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{N(s,a)}\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)}+\frac{48\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)}+\frac{2}{N(1-\gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{48\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{N(s,a)}\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)}+\sqrt{\frac{96\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}}+\frac{48\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)}+\frac{2}{N(1-\gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{48\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{N(s,a)}\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)}+\frac{60\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)},

(161)

where the second line holds since

\Big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}-\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)\Big|\leq\big(\big\|\widehat{P}_{s,a}\big\|_{1}+\big\|P_{s,a}\big\|_{1}\big)\big\|\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}-\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2}{N(1-\gamma)},

the penultimate line is valid due to (
160
), and the last line holds true under the conditions that
T\geq N(s,a)
and that
T
is sufficiently large. Moreover, apply (
159b
) and the triangle inequality to arrive at

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)\leq\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)+\Big|\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)-\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\leq}2\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)+\frac{5\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}+\frac{2}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)+2\Big|\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)-\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u_{0}}^{\star}\big)\Big|+\frac{5\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}+\frac{2}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}2\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big)+\frac{6}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N}+\frac{5\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big)+\frac{23\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)},

(162)

where (i) arise from (
159b
) and (
160
), (ii) follows from (
160
), and the last line holds true since
N\geq N(s,a)
.

Step 5: extending the bounds to
\widetilde{V}
.

Consider any
\widetilde{V}
obeying
\|\widetilde{V}-\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{N}
and
\|\widetilde{V}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
.
Invoke (
161
) and the triangle inequality to arrive at

\displaystyle\Big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)\widetilde{V}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\Big|+\Big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)\big(\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}-\widetilde{V}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{48\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{N(s,a)}\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)}+\frac{60\log\frac{2N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)}+\frac{2}{N},

\displaystyle\leq 12\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{2N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{N(s,a)}\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{s,u^{\star}}^{\star}\big)}+\frac{62\log\frac{2N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)}

\displaystyle=12\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{2N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{N(s,a)}\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big)}+\frac{62\log\frac{2N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)},

(163)

where the penultimate inequality relies on
N\geq N(s,a)
, and the second line holds since

\Big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)\big(\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}-\widetilde{V}\big)\Big|\leq\big(\big\|\widehat{P}_{s,a}\big\|_{1}+\big\|P_{s,a}\big\|_{1}\big)\big\|\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}-\widetilde{V}\big\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2}{N}.

Given that
\big\|\widetilde{V}-\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}\leq 1/N
,
we can repeat the argument for (
160
) allows one to demonstrate that

\Big|\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widehat{V}_{\mathsf{pe}}^{\star}\big)-\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widetilde{V}\big)\Big|\leq\frac{2}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N}

which taken together with (
163
) and the basic inequality
\sqrt{x+y}\leq\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y}
gives

\displaystyle\Big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)\widetilde{V}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 12\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{2N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{N(s,a)}\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widetilde{V}\big)}+12\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{2N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{N(s,a)}\cdot\frac{2}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N}}+\frac{62\log\frac{2N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)}

\displaystyle\leq 12\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{2N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{N(s,a)}\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widetilde{V}\big)}+\frac{74\log\frac{2N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)}.

Additionally, repeating the argument for (
162
) leads to another desired inequality:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}\big(\widetilde{V}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widetilde{V}\big)+\frac{6}{(1-\gamma)N}+\frac{23\log\frac{8N^{3}}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}\big(\widetilde{V}\big)+\frac{41\log\frac{2N}{(1-\gamma)\delta}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}.

A.4.1
Proof of Lemma
9

In this proof, we shalle often use
\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}
to abbreviate
\mathsf{Var}_{P_{s,a}}
for notational simplicity.
Before proceeding, let us define the following vector

\displaystyle\overline{V}=V-(P_{s,a}V)1,

(164)

with
1
denoting the all-one vector. It is clearly seen that

\displaystyle P_{s,a}\big(\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\big)

\displaystyle=P_{s,a}\big(V\circ V\big)-\big(P_{s,a}V\big)^{2}=\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V).

(165)

In addition, we make note of the following basic facts that will prove useful:

\displaystyle\|V\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma},\qquad\|\overline{V}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma},\qquad\|\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\overline{V}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq H^{2},

(166a)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}\big(\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\big)

\displaystyle\leq P_{s,a}\big(\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\big)\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}P_{s,a}\big(\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\big)=\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}\big(V\big).

(166b)

Proof of inequality (
150a
).

If
0<N(s,a)<48\log\frac{N}{\delta}
, then we can immediately see that

\displaystyle\left|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)V\right|\leq\|V\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\leq\frac{48\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)},

(167)

and hence the claim (
150a
) is valid. As a result, it suffices to focus on the case where

\displaystyle N(s,a)\geq 48\log\frac{N}{\delta}.

(168)

Note that the total number of pairs
(s,a)
with nonzero
N(s,a)
cannot exceed
N
.
Akin to (
148
), taking the Bernstein inequality together with (
166
) and invoking the union bound, we can demonstrate that with probability at least
1-4\delta
,

\displaystyle\left|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)V\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{4\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{N(s,a)}}+\frac{2\|V\|_{\infty}\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{3N(s,a)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{4\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{N(s,a)}}+\frac{2\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)N(s,a)}

(169a)

\displaystyle\left|\big(P_{s,a}-\widehat{P}_{s,a}\big)\big(\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\big)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{4\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}\big(\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\big)\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{N(s,a)}}+\frac{2\|\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\|_{\infty}\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{3N(s,a)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{4\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}}+\frac{2\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}

(169b)

hold simultaneously over all
(s,a)
with
N(s,a)>0
.
Note, however, that the Bernstein bounds in (
169
) involve the variance
\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)
;
we still need to connect
\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)
with its empirical estimate
\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}(V)
.

In the sequel, let us look at two cases separately.

•

Case 1:
\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)\leq\frac{9\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}
.

In this case, our bound (
169a
) immediately leads to

\displaystyle\left|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)V\right|\leq\frac{7\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)}.

(170)

•

Case 2:
\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)>\frac{9\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}
.

We first single out the following useful identity:

\displaystyle\widehat{P}_{s,a}\big(\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\big)-\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}(V)=\widehat{P}_{s,a}\big(\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\big)-\left[\widehat{P}_{s,a}\big(V\circ V\big)-\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}V\big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\widehat{P}_{s,a}\big(V\circ V\big)-2\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}V\big)\big(P_{s,a}V\big)+\big(P_{s,a}V\big)^{2}-\left[\widehat{P}_{s,a}\big(V\circ V\big)-\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}V\big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)V\big|^{2}.

(171)

Combining (
171
) with (
169b
) then implies that, with probability exceeding
1-4\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)=P_{s,a}\big(\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\big)=\big(P_{s,a}-\widehat{P}_{s,a}\big)\big(\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\big)+\widehat{P}_{s,a}\big(\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\big)

\displaystyle=\big(P_{s,a}-\widehat{P}_{s,a}\big)\big(\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\big)+\left\{\big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)V\big|^{2}+\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}(V)\right\}

(172)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{4\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}}\sqrt{\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)}+\big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)V\big|^{2}+\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}(V)+\frac{2\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{3}\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)+\big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)V\big|^{2}+\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}(V)+\frac{2\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)},

(173)

where the second line arises from the identity (
171
),
the penultimate inequality results from (
169b
),
and the last inequality holds true due to the assumption
\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)>\frac{9\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}
in this case.
Rearranging terms of the above inequality, we are left with

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)

\displaystyle\leq 3\big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)V\big|^{2}+3\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}(V)+\frac{2\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}

Taking this upper bound on
\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)
collectively with (
169a
) and using a little algebra lead to

\displaystyle\left|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)V\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{12\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{N(s,a)}}\big|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)V\big|+\sqrt{\frac{12\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}(V)\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{N(s,a)}}+\frac{5\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)}

(174)

with probability at least
1-4\delta
.
When
N(s,a)\geq 48\log\frac{N}{\delta}
(cf. (
168
)),
one has
\sqrt{\frac{12\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{N(s,a)}}\leq 1/2
.
Substituting this into (
174
) and rearranging terms, we arrive at

\displaystyle\left|\big(\widehat{P}_{s,a}-P_{s,a}\big)V\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{48\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}(V)\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{N(s,a)}}+\frac{10\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)N(s,a)}

with probability at least
1-4\delta
.

Putting the above two cases together establishes the advertised bound (
150a
).

Proof of inequality (
150b
).

It follows from (
172
) and (
169a
) that with probability at least
1-4\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)

\displaystyle\geq-\left|\big(P_{s,a}-\widehat{P}_{s,a}\big)\big(\overline{V}\circ\overline{V}\big)\right|+\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}(V)

\displaystyle\geq-\sqrt{\frac{4\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}\big(V\big)\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}}-\frac{2\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}+\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}(V),

or equivalently,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}(V)

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)+2\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}\big(V\big)\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}}+\frac{2\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}.

Invoke the elementary inequality
2xy\leq x^{2}+y^{2}
to establish the claimed bound:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}_{s,a}}(V)

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)+\left(\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}\big(V\big)+\frac{\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}\right)+\frac{2\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}

\displaystyle=2\mathsf{Var}_{s,a}(V)+\frac{5\log\frac{N}{\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)^{2}N(s,a)}.

Appendix B
Proof of auxiliary lemmas: episodic finite-horizon MDPs

B.1
Proof of Lemma
3

(a) Let us begin by proving the claim (
57a
). Recall from our construction that
\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{aux}}
is composed of the second half of the sample trajectories, and hence for each
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
1\leq h\leq H
,

N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)=\sum_{k=K/2+1}^{K}\mathds{1}\big\{s_{h}^{k}=s\big\}

can be viewed as the sum of
K/2
independent Bernoulli random variables, each with mean
d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)
.
According to the union bound and the Bernstein inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists(s,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times[H]:\left|N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)-\frac{K}{2}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\right|\geq\tau\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S},h\in[H]}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)-\frac{K}{2}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\right|\geq\tau\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2SH\exp\left(-\frac{\tau^{2}/2}{v_{s,h}+\tau/3}\right)

for any
\tau\geq 0
, where

v_{s,h}\coloneqq\frac{K}{2}\mathsf{Var}\big(\mathds{1}\{s_{h}^{t}=s\}\big)=\frac{Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\big(1-d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\big)}{2}\leq\frac{Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)}{2}.

A little algebra then yields that with probability at least
1-2\delta
, one has

\displaystyle\left|N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)-\frac{K}{2}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{4v_{s,h}\log\frac{HS}{\delta}}+\frac{2}{3}\log\frac{HS}{\delta}\leq\sqrt{2Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\log\frac{HS}{\delta}}+\log\frac{HS}{\delta}

(175)

simultaneously for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and all
1\leq h\leq H
.
The same argument also reveals that with probability exceeding
1-2\delta
,

\displaystyle\left|N^{\mathsf{main}}_{h}(s)-\frac{K}{2}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\right|\leq\sqrt{2Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\log\frac{HS}{\delta}}+\log\frac{HS}{\delta}

(176)

holds simultaneously for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and all
1\leq h\leq H
.
Combine (
175
) and (
176
) to show that

\displaystyle\left|N^{\mathsf{main}}_{h}(s)-N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)\right|\leq 2\sqrt{2Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\log\frac{HS}{\delta}}+2\log\frac{HS}{\delta}

(177)

for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and all
1\leq h\leq H
.

To establish the claimed result (
57a
), we divide into two cases.

•

Case 1:
N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)\leq 100\log\frac{HS}{\delta}
.
By construction, it is easily seen that

\displaystyle N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)=\max\left\{N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)-10\sqrt{N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)\log\frac{HS}{\delta}},\,0\right\}=0\leq N^{\mathsf{main}}_{h}(s).

(178)

•

Case 2:
N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)>100\log\frac{HS}{\delta}
.
In this case, invoking (
175
) reveals that

\frac{K}{2}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)+\sqrt{2Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\log\frac{HS}{\delta}}+\log\frac{HS}{\delta}\geq N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)>100\log\frac{HS}{\delta},

and hence one necessarily has

\displaystyle Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\geq(9\sqrt{2})^{2}\log\frac{HS}{\delta}\geq 100\log\frac{HS}{\delta}.

(179)

In turn, this property (
179
) taken collectively with (
148
) ensures that

\displaystyle N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)\geq\frac{K}{2}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)-\sqrt{2Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\log\frac{HS}{\delta}}-\log\frac{HS}{\delta}\geq\frac{K}{4}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s).

(180)

Therefore, in the case with
N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)>100\log\frac{HS}{\delta}
,
we can demonstrate that

\displaystyle N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)

\displaystyle=\max\left\{N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)-10\sqrt{N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)\log\frac{HS}{\delta}},\,0\right\}=N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)-10\sqrt{N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)\log\frac{HS}{\delta}}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)-5\sqrt{Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\log\frac{HS}{\delta}}\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\leq}N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)-\left\{2\sqrt{2Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\log\frac{HS}{\delta}}+2\log\frac{HS}{\delta}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(iii)}}{\leq}N^{\mathsf{main}}_{h}(s),

(181)

where (i) comes from Condition (
180
), (ii) is valid under the condition (
179
),
and (iii) holds true with probability at least
1-2\delta
due to the inequality (
177
).

Putting the above two cases together establishes the claim (
57a
).

(b) We now turn to the second claim (
57b
).
Towards this, we first claim that the following bound holds simultaneously for all
(s,a,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times[H]
with probability exceeding
1-2\delta
:

\displaystyle N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s,a)\geq N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)-\sqrt{4N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}-\log\frac{KH}{\delta}.

(182)

Let us take this claim as given for the moment, and return to establish it towards the end of this section.
We shall discuss the following two cases separately.

•

If
Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s,a)=Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)>1600\log\frac{KH}{\delta}
,
then it follows from (
180
) (with slight modification) that

\displaystyle N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)\geq\frac{K}{4}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\geq 400\log\frac{KH}{\delta}.

(183)

This property together with the definition of
N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)
in turn allows us to derive

\displaystyle N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)

\displaystyle\geq N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)-10\sqrt{N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}\geq\frac{K}{4}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)-10\sqrt{\frac{K}{4}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{K}{8}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s),

and as a result,

\displaystyle N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{K}{8}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)=\frac{K}{8}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s,a)\geq 200\log\frac{KH}{\delta},

where the last inequality arises from the assumption of this case.
Taking this lower bound with (
182
) implies that

\displaystyle N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s,a)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{K}{8}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s,a)-\sqrt{\frac{K}{2}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s,a)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}-\log\frac{KH}{\delta}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{K}{8}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s,a)-2\sqrt{Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s,a)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}.

•

If
Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s,a)\leq 1600\log\frac{KH}{\delta}
, then one can easily verify that

\frac{K}{8}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s,a)-5\sqrt{Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s,a)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}\leq 0\leq N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s,a).

Putting these two cases together concludes the proof, provided that the claim (
182
) is valid.

Proof of inequality (
182
).

Let us look at two cases separately.

•

If
N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)\leq 4\log\frac{KH}{\delta}
, then
the right-hand side of (
182
) is negative, and hence the claim (
182
) holds trivially.

•

We then turn attention to the following set:

\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{large}}\coloneqq\bigg\{(s,a,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times[H]\,\,\Big|\,\,N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)>4\log\frac{KH}{\delta}\bigg\}.

(184)

Recognizing that

\displaystyle\sum_{(s,a,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times[H]}N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(s,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times[H]}N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)=\sum_{(s,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times[H]}N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(s,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times[H]}N^{\mathsf{aux}}_{h}(s)=\frac{KH}{2},

we can immediately bound the cardinality of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{large}}
as follows:

\big|\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{large}}\big|<\frac{\sum_{(s,a,h)}N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)}{4\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}\leq KH/2.

(185)

Additionally, it follows from our construction that: conditional on
N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)
,
N^{\mathsf{main}}_{h}(s)
and the high-probability event (
57a
),

N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s,a)
can be viewed as the sum of
\min\big\{N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s),N^{\mathsf{main}}_{h}(s)\big\}=N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)

independent Bernoulli random variables each with mean
\pi_{h}(a\,|\,s)
.
As a result, repeating the Bernstein-type argument in (
148
) on the event (
57a
)
reveals that, with probability at least
1-2\delta/(KH)
,

\displaystyle N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s,a)\geq N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)-\sqrt{4N^{\mathsf{trim}}_{h}(s)\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}-\log\frac{KH}{\delta}

(186)

for any fixed triple
(s,a,h)
. Taking the union bound over all
(s,a,h)\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{large}}
and using the bound (
185
) imply that
with probability exceeding
1-\delta
,
(
186
) holds simultaneously for all
(s,a,h)\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{large}}
.

Combining the above two cases allows one to conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta
,
the advertised property (
182
)
holds simultaneously for all
(s,a,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times[H]
.

B.2
Proof of the instance-dependent statistical bound (
65
)

To establish relation (
65
), we make use of relation (171) as follows: for any
1\leq h\leq H
,

\displaystyle\big\langle d_{h}^{\star},V_{h}^{\star}-V_{h}^{\widehat{\pi}}\big\rangle\leq 2\sum_{j=h}^{H}\big\langle d_{j}^{\star},b_{j}^{\star}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{j=h}^{H}\sum_{s}d_{j}^{\star}(s)\sqrt{\frac{32c_{\mathsf{b}}\log\frac{NH}{\delta}}{Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{j}\big(s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)\big)}\mathsf{Var}_{P_{j,s,\pi^{\star}_{j}(s)}}(\widehat{V}_{j+1})}+\frac{192c_{\mathsf{b}}H^{2}SC^{\star}_{\mathsf{clipped}}\log\frac{NH}{\delta}}{K}

\displaystyle\leq 12\sum_{j=h}^{H}\sum_{s}d_{j}^{\star}(s)\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mathsf{b}}\log\frac{NH}{\delta}}{Kd^{\mathsf{b}}_{j}\big(s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)\big)}\mathsf{Var}_{P_{j,s,\pi^{\star}_{j}(s)}}(V_{j+1}^{\star})}+20\Big(\frac{c_{\mathsf{b}}H^{3}SC^{\star}\log\frac{NH}{\delta}}{K}\Big)^{3/4},

(187)

provided that
K\geq 100c_{\mathsf{b}}HSC^{\star}\log\frac{NH}{\delta}
.
To see why the last inequality in (
187
) holds, it suffices to observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{s}d_{j}^{\star}(s)\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{Var}_{P_{j,s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)}}(\widehat{V}_{j+1})}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{j}\big(s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)\big)}}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}\sum_{s}d_{j}^{\star}(s)\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{Var}_{P_{j,s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)}}(V_{j+1}^{\star})}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{j}\big(s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)\big)}}+\sum_{s}d_{j}^{\star}(s)\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{Var}_{P_{j,s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)}}(\widehat{V}_{j+1}-V_{j+1}^{\star})}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{j}\big(s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)\big)}}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\leq}\sum_{s}d_{j}^{\star}(s)\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{Var}_{P_{j,s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)}}(V_{j+1}^{\star})}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{j}\big(s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)\big)}}+\sum_{s}d_{j}^{\star}(s)\sqrt{\frac{H\cdot\big\langle P_{j,s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)},\widehat{V}_{j+1}-V_{j+1}^{\star}\big\rangle}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{j}\big(s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)\big)}}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(iii)}}{\leq}\sum_{s}d_{j}^{\star}(s)\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{Var}_{P_{j,s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)}}(V_{j+1}^{\star})}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{j}\big(s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)\big)}}+\sqrt{S}\sqrt{H\sum_{s}\frac{\big(d_{j}^{\star}(s)\big)^{2}}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{j}\big(s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)\big)}\big\langle P_{j,s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)},\widehat{V}_{j+1}-V_{j+1}^{\star}\big\rangle}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(iv)}}{\leq}\sum_{s}d_{j}^{\star}(s)\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{Var}_{P_{j,s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)}}(V_{j+1}^{\star})}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{j}\big(s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)\big)}}+\sqrt{HSC^{\star}\sum_{s}d_{j}^{\star}(s)\big\langle P_{j,s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)},\widehat{V}_{j+1}-V_{j+1}^{\star}\big\rangle}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s}d_{j}^{\star}(s)\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{Var}_{P_{j,s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)}}(V_{j+1}^{\star})}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{j}\big(s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)\big)}}+\sqrt{HSC^{\star}\sum_{s}d_{j+1}^{\star}(s)\big[V_{j+1}^{\star}(s)-\widehat{V}_{j+1}(s)\big]}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(v)}}{\leq}\sum_{s}d_{j}^{\star}(s)\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{Var}_{P_{j,s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)}}(V_{j+1}^{\star})}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{j}\big(s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)\big)}}+\sqrt{HSC^{\star}\cdot 80\sqrt{\frac{2c_{\mathsf{b}}H^{3}SC^{\star}\log\frac{NH}{\delta}}{K}}},

(188)

where (i) holds due to the elementary inequality
\sqrt{\mathsf{Var}(X+Y)}\leq\sqrt{\mathsf{Var}(X)}+\sqrt{\mathsf{Var}(Y)}
;
(ii) follows since

\mathsf{Var}_{P_{j,s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)}}(\widehat{V}_{j+1}-V_{j+1}^{\star})\leq\big\langle P_{j,s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)},(\widehat{V}_{j+1}-V_{j+1}^{\star})^{2}\big\rangle\leq H\big\langle P_{j,s,\pi_{j}^{\star}(s)},\widehat{V}_{j+1}-V_{j+1}^{\star}\big\rangle,

which comes from the fact that
V_{j+1}^{\star}\geq\widehat{V}_{j+1}\geq 0
and
\|V_{j+1}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq H
; (iii) invokes the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality;
(iv) makes use of the definition of
C^{\star}_{\mathsf{clipped}}
; and (v) is obtained by applying (
116
) of Theorem
6
.

Appendix C
Proof of minimax lower bounds

C.1
Preliminary facts

For any two distributions
P
and
Q
,
we denote by
\mathsf{KL}(P\parallel Q)
the Kullback-Leibler (KL) divergence of
P
and
Q
.
Letting
\mathsf{Ber}(p)
be the Bernoulli distribution with mean
p
,
we also introduce

\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(p\parallel q)\coloneqq p\log\frac{p}{q}+(1-p)\log\frac{1-p}{1-q}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\chi^{2}(p\parallel q)\coloneqq\frac{(p-q)^{2}}{q}+\frac{(p-q)^{2}}{1-q},

(189)

which represent respectively the KL divergence and the chi-square divergence of
\mathsf{Ber}(p)
from
\mathsf{Ber}(q)

(
Tsybakov, 2009
)
.
We make note of the following useful properties about the KL divergence.

Lemma 10
.

For any
p,q\in\left[\frac{1}{2},1\right)
and
p>q
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(p\parallel q)\leq\mathsf{KL}(q\parallel p)\leq\chi^{2}(q\parallel p)=\frac{(p-q)^{2}}{p(1-p)}.

(190)

Proof.

The second inequality in (
190
) is a well-known relation between KL divergence and chi-square divergence; see
Tsybakov, 2009
.
As a result, it suffices to justify the first inequality.
Towards this end, let us introduce
a=\frac{p+q}{2}\in\big[\frac{1}{2},1\big]
and

b=\frac{p-q}{2}\in\big[0,\frac{1}{4}\big]
, which allow us to re-parameterize
(p,q)
as

p=a+b
and
q=a-b
. The definition (
189
) together with a little algebra gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(p\parallel q)-\mathsf{KL}(q\parallel p)

\displaystyle=(p+q)\log\frac{p}{q}+(2-p-q)\log\frac{1-p}{1-q}

\displaystyle=2a\log\left(\frac{a+b}{a-b}\right)+2(1-a)\log\frac{1-a-b}{1-a+b}\eqqcolon g(a,b).

Taking the derivative w.r.t.
b
yields

\frac{\partial g(a,b)}{\partial b}=2a\left\{\frac{1}{a+b}+\frac{1}{a-b}\right\}-2\left(1-a\right)\left\{\frac{1}{1-a+b}+\frac{1}{1-a-b}\right\}=f(a)-f(1-a)\leq 0,

with
f(x)\coloneqq\frac{2x}{x+b}+\frac{2x}{x-b}
(for
x>b
).
Here, the last inequality follows since
f(\cdot)
is a decreasing function and that
a\geq 1-a
.
This implies that
g(a,b)
is non-increasing in
b\geq 0
for any given
a
, which in turn leads to

\mathsf{KL}(p\parallel q)-\mathsf{KL}(q\parallel p)=g(a,b)\leq g(a,0)=0

as claimed.
∎

C.2
Proof of Theorem
2

We now construct some hard problem instances and use them to establish the minimax lower bounds claimed in Theorem
2
.
It is assumed throughout this subsection that

\frac{2}{3}\leq\gamma<1\qquad\text{and}\qquad\frac{14(1-\gamma)\varepsilon}{\gamma}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(191)

C.2.1
Construction of hard problem instances

Construction of the hard MDPs.

Let us introduce two MDPs
\left\{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\theta},r,\gamma)\mid\theta\in\{0,1\}\right\}

parameterized by
\theta
, which involve
S
states and 2 actions as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}=\{0,1,\ldots,S-1\}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{A}=\{0,1\}.

We single out a crucial state distribution (supported on the state subset
\{0,1\}
) as follows:

\displaystyle\mu(s)=\frac{1}{CS}\mathds{1}\{s=0\}+\Big(1-\frac{1}{CS}\Big)\mathds{1}\{s=1\}

(192)

for some quantity
C>0
obeying

\frac{1}{CS}\leq\frac{1}{4\gamma}.

(193)

We shall make clear the relation between
C
and the concentrability coefficient
C^{\star}_{\mathsf{clipped}}
shortly (see (
210
)).
Armed with this distribution, we are ready to define the transition kernel
P_{\theta}
of the MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
as follows:

\displaystyle P_{\theta}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}p\mathds{1}\{s^{\prime}=0\}+(1-p)\mu(s^{\prime})&\text{for}&(s,a)=(0,\theta),\\
q\mathds{1}\{s^{\prime}=0\}+(1-q)\mu(s^{\prime})&\text{for}&(s,a)=(0,1-\theta),\\
\mathds{1}\{s^{\prime}=1\}&\text{for}&(s,a)=(1,0),\\
(2\gamma-1)\mathds{1}\{s^{\prime}=1\}+2(1-\gamma)\mu(s^{\prime})&\text{for}&(s,a)=(1,1),\\
\gamma\mathds{1}(s^{\prime}=s\}+(1-\gamma)\mu(s^{\prime})&\text{for}&s>1,\end{array}\right.

where the parameters
p
and
q
are chosen to be

\displaystyle p=\gamma+\frac{14(1-\gamma)^{2}\varepsilon}{\gamma},\qquad q=\gamma-\frac{14(1-\gamma)^{2}\varepsilon}{\gamma}.

(199)

In view of the assumptions (
191
), one has

p>q\geq\gamma-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}.

(200)

As can be clearly seen from the construction, if the MDP is initialized to either state 0 or state 1, then it will never leave the state subset
\{0,1\}
.
In addition, the reward function for any MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
is chosen to be

\displaystyle r(s,a)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}1&\text{for}&s=0,\\
\frac{1}{2}&\text{for}&(s,a)=(1,0),\\
0&\text{for}&(s,a)=(1,1),\\
0&\text{for}&s>1,\end{array}\right.

where the reward gained in state 0 is clearly higher than that in other states.

Value functions and optimal policies.

Next, let us take a moment to compute the value functions of the constructed MDPs and identify the optimal policies.
For notational clarity, for the MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
with
\theta\in\{0,1\}
,
we denote by
\pi_{\theta}^{\star}
the optimal policy,
and let
V_{\theta}^{\pi}
(resp.
V_{\theta}^{\star}
) represent the value function of policy
\pi
(resp.
\pi_{\theta}^{\star}
).
The lemma below collects several useful properties about the value functions and the optimal policies; the proof is deferred to Appendix
C.2.3
.

Lemma 11
.

Consider any
\theta\in\{0,1\}
and any policy
\pi
. One has

\displaystyle V_{\theta}^{\pi}(0)=\frac{1+\gamma(1-x_{\pi,\theta})\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)}{1-\gamma\big(\mu(1)x_{\pi,\theta}+\mu(0)\big)}=V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)+\frac{1-(1-\gamma)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)}{1-\gamma\mu(0)-\gamma\mu(1)x_{\pi,\theta}},

(205)

where we define

\displaystyle x_{\pi,\theta}\coloneqq p\pi(\theta\,|\,0)+q\pi(1-\theta\,|\,0).

(206)

In addition, the optimal policy
\pi_{\theta}^{\star}
and the optimal value function obey

\displaystyle\pi_{\theta}^{\star}(\theta\,|\,0)=1,\qquad\pi^{\star}_{\theta}(0\,|\,1)=1,\qquad\text{and}\qquad V^{\star}_{\theta}(1)=\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)}.

(207)

Construction of the batch dataset.

Given any constructed MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
, we generate a dataset containing
N
i.i.d. samples
\{(s_{i},a_{i},s_{i}^{\prime})\}_{1\leq i\leq N}
according to (
17
),
where the initial state distribution
\rho^{\mathsf{b}}
and behavior policy
\pi^{\mathsf{b}}
are chosen to be:

\displaystyle\rho^{\mathsf{b}}(s)=\mu(s)\qquad\text{and }\qquad\pi^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)=1/2,\qquad\forall(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A},

with
\mu
denoting the distribution defined in (
192
).
Interestingly, the occupancy state distribution of this dataset coincides with
\mu
, in the sense that

\displaystyle d^{\mathsf{b}}(s)=\mu(s)\qquad\text{and}\qquad d^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)=\mu(s)/2,\qquad\forall(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}.

(208)

Moreover, letting us choose the test distribution
\rho
in a way that

\displaystyle\rho(s)=\begin{cases}1,\quad&\text{if }s=0\\
0,&\text{if }s>0.\end{cases}

(209)

we can also characterize the single-policy clipped concentrability coefficient
C^{\star}_{\mathsf{clipped}}
of the dataset w.r.t. the constructed MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
as follows

\displaystyle C^{\star}_{\mathsf{clipped}}=2C.

(210)

The proof of the claims (
208
) and (
210
) can be found in Appendix
C.2.4
.

C.2.2
Establishing the minimax lower bound

Equipped with the above construction, we are ready to develop our lower bounds.
We remind the reader of the test distribution
\rho
chosen in (
209
),
and hence we need to control
\langle\rho,V_{\theta}^{\star}-V_{\theta}^{\widehat{\pi}}\rangle=V_{\theta}^{\star}(0)-V_{\theta}^{\widehat{\pi}}(0)
with
\widehat{\pi}
representing a policy estimate (computed based on the batch dataset).

Step 1: converting
\widehat{\pi}
into an estimate
\widehat{\theta}
of
\theta
.

Consider first an arbitrary policy
\pi
.
By combining the definition (
206
) with the properties (
207
), we see that
x_{\pi_{\theta}^{\star},\theta}=p
,
which together with (
205
) gives

\displaystyle\langle\rho,V_{\theta}^{\star}-V_{\theta}^{\pi}\rangle

\displaystyle=V_{\theta}^{\star}(0)-V_{\theta}^{\pi}(0)=\frac{1+\gamma(1-p)\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)}{1-\gamma\big(\mu(1)p+\mu(0)\big)}-\frac{1+\gamma(1-x_{\pi,\theta})\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)}{1-\gamma\big(\mu(1)x_{\pi,\theta}+\mu(0)\big)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1+\gamma(1-p)\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)}{1-\gamma\big(\mu(1)p+\mu(0)\big)}-\frac{1+\gamma(1-x_{\pi,\theta})\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)}{1-\gamma\big(\mu(1)x_{\pi,\theta}+\mu(0)\big)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{21\varepsilon}{8}\big(1-\pi(\theta\,|\,0)\big).

(211)

Here, the second line holds since
V_{\theta}^{\pi}\leq V_{\theta}^{\star}
,
and the last inequality will be established in Appendix
C.2.4
.

Denoting by
\mathbb{P}_{\theta}
the probability distribution when the MDP is
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
, suppose for the moment that the policy estimate
\widehat{\pi}
achieves

\mathbb{P}_{\theta}\big\{\big\langle\rho,V_{\theta}^{\star}-V_{\theta}^{\widehat{\pi}}\big\rangle\leq\varepsilon\big\}\geq\frac{7}{8},

then in view of (
211
), one necessarily has
\widehat{\pi}(\theta\,|\,0)\geq\frac{13}{21}
with probability at least
7/8
.
If this were true, then we could then construct the following estimate
\widehat{\theta}
for
\theta
:

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}=\arg\max_{a}\,\widehat{\pi}(a\,|\,0),

(212)

which would necessarily satisfy

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta}\big(\widehat{\theta}=\theta\big)\geq\mathbb{P}_{\theta}\big\{\widehat{\pi}(\theta\,|\,0)>1/2\big\}\geq\mathbb{P}_{\theta}\bigg\{\widehat{\pi}(\theta\,|\,0)\geq\frac{13}{21}\bigg\}\geq\frac{7}{8}.

(213)

In what follows,
we would like to show that (
213
) cannot happen — i.e., one cannot possibly find such a good estimator for
\theta
— without a sufficient number of samples.

Step 2: probability of error in testing two hypotheses.

The next step lies in studying the feasibility of differentiating two hypotheses
\theta=0
and
\theta=1
.
Define the minimax probability of error as follows

p_{\mathrm{e}}\coloneqq\inf_{\psi}\max\big\{\mathbb{P}_{0}(\psi\neq 0),\,\mathbb{P}_{1}(\psi\neq 1)\big\},

(214)

where the infimum is taken over all possible tests
\psi
(based on the batch dataset in hand).
Letting
\mu^{\mathsf{b}}_{\theta}
denote the distribution of a sample
(s_{i},a_{i},s_{i}^{\prime})
under the MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}

and recalling that the samples are independently generated,
one can demonstrate that

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{e}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\exp\Big(-N\mathsf{KL}\big(\mu^{\mathsf{b}}_{0}\parallel\mu^{\mathsf{b}}_{1}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\exp\bigg\{-\frac{1}{2}N\mu(0)\Big(\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{0}(\cdot\,|\,0,0)\parallel P_{1}(\cdot\,|\,0,0)\big)+\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{0}(\cdot\,|\,0,1)\parallel P_{1}(\cdot\,|\,0,1)\big)\Big)\bigg\}.

(215)

Here, the first inequality results from
Tsybakov, 2009
and the additivity property of the KL divergence (cf.
Tsybakov, 2009
),
and the second line holds true since

\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(\mu^{\mathsf{b}}_{0}\parallel\mu^{\mathsf{b}}_{1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s,a,s^{\prime}}\mu(s)\pi^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)P_{0}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)\log\frac{\mu(s)\pi^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)P_{0}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)}{\mu(s)\pi^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)P_{1}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mu(0)\sum_{a}\sum_{s^{\prime}}P_{0}(s^{\prime}\,|\,0,a)\log\frac{P_{0}(s^{\prime}\,|\,0,a)}{P_{1}(s^{\prime}\,|\,0,a)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mu(0)\sum_{a}\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{0}(\cdot\,|\,0,a)\parallel P_{1}(\cdot\,|\,0,a)\big),

where the second line is valid since
P_{0}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)
and
P_{1}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)
differ only when
s=0
.

Next, we turn attention to the KL divergence of interest. Recall that

\displaystyle P_{0}(0\,|\,0,0)=\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)p+\frac{1}{CS},\qquad P_{1}(0\,|\,0,0)=\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)q+\frac{1}{CS}.

Given that
p\geq q\geq 1/2
(see (
200
)),
we can apply Lemma
10
to arrive at

\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{0}(\cdot\,|\,0,0)\parallel P_{1}(\cdot\,|\,0,0)\big)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{KL}\left(\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)p+\frac{1}{CS}\parallel\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)q+\frac{1}{CS}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}\frac{\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)^{2}(p-q)^{2}}{\left(\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)p+\frac{1}{CS}\right)\left(1-p-(1-p)\frac{1}{CS}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)^{2}(p-q)^{2}}{p\left((1-p)\big(1-\frac{1}{CS}\big)\right)}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{=}\frac{784(1-\gamma)^{4}\varepsilon^{2}}{\gamma^{2}\left(\gamma+\frac{14(1-\gamma)^{2}\varepsilon}{\gamma}\right)\left(1-\gamma-\frac{14(1-\gamma)^{2}\varepsilon}{\gamma}\right)}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{\leq}\frac{1568(1-\gamma)^{4}\varepsilon^{2}}{\gamma^{3}(1-\gamma)}\overset{(\mathrm{iv})}{\leq}12544(1-\gamma)^{3}\varepsilon^{2},

where (i) arises from Lemma
10
,
(ii) follows from the definitions of
p
and
q
(
199
),
(iii) holds true as long as
\frac{14(1-\gamma)^{2}\varepsilon}{\gamma}\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{2}
, and (iv) results from the assumption
\gamma\in[\frac{1}{2},1)
.
Evidently, the same upper bound holds for
\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{0}(\cdot\,|\,0,1)\parallel P_{1}(\cdot\,|\,0,1)\big)
as well.
Substitution back into (
215
) reveals that:
if the sample size does not exceed

\displaystyle N\leq\frac{CS\log 2}{12544(1-\gamma)^{3}\varepsilon^{2}}=\frac{C^{\star}_{\mathsf{clipped}}S\log 2}{25088(1-\gamma)^{3}\varepsilon^{2}},

(216)

then one necessarily has

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{e}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\exp\Big(-12544N\mu(0)(1-\gamma)^{3}\varepsilon^{2}\Big)=\frac{1}{4}\exp\Big(-\frac{12544N(1-\gamma)^{3}\varepsilon^{2}}{CS}\Big)\geq\frac{1}{8}.

(217)

Step 3: putting all this together.

To finish up, suppose that there exists an estimator
\widehat{\pi}
such that

\mathbb{P}_{0}\big\{\big\langle\rho,V_{0}^{\star}-V_{0}^{\widehat{\pi}}\big\rangle>\varepsilon\big\}<\frac{1}{8}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbb{P}_{1}\big\{\big\langle\rho,V_{0}^{\star}-V_{0}^{\widehat{\pi}}\big\rangle>\varepsilon\big\}<\frac{1}{8}.

Then in view of our arguments in Step 1, the estimator
\widehat{\theta}
defined in (
212
) must satisfy

\mathbb{P}_{0}\big(\widehat{\theta}\neq\theta\big)<\frac{1}{8}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbb{P}_{1}\big(\widehat{\theta}\neq\theta\big)<\frac{1}{8}.

This, however, cannot possibly happen under the sample size condition (
216
); otherwise it contradicts the lower bound (
217
).

C.2.3
Proof of Lemma
11

To begin with, for any policy
\pi
, the value function of state
0
obeys

\displaystyle V_{\theta}^{\pi}(0)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{a\sim\pi(\cdot\,|\,0)}\bigg[r(0,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}P_{\theta}(s^{\prime}\,|\,0,a)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(s^{\prime})\bigg]

\displaystyle=1+\gamma\pi(\theta\,|\,0)\Big[\big(p+(1-p)\mu(0)\big)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(0)+(1-p)\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+\gamma\pi(1-\theta\,|\,0)\Big[\big(q+(1-q)\mu(0)\big)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(0)+(1-q)\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)\Big]

\displaystyle=1+\gamma\Big[p\pi(\theta\,|\,0)+q\pi(1-\theta\,|\,0)+\mu(0)-p\pi(\theta\,|\,0)\mu(0)-q\pi(1-\theta\,|\,0)\mu(0)\Big]V_{\theta}^{\pi}(0)

\displaystyle\qquad+\gamma\mu(1)\Big[1-p\pi(\theta\,|\,0)-q\pi(1-\theta\,|\,0)\Big]V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{=}1+\gamma\Big[x_{\pi,\theta}+(1-x_{\pi,\theta})\mu(0)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(0)+(1-x_{\pi,\theta})\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{=}1+\gamma\Big[\big(\mu(1)x_{\pi,\theta}+\mu(0)\big)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(0)+(1-x_{\pi,\theta})\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)\Big],

(218)

where in (i) we have defined the following quantity

\displaystyle x_{\pi,\theta}=p\pi(\theta\,|\,0)+q\pi(1-\theta\,|\,0)=q+(p-q)\pi(\theta\,|\,0),

(219)

and (ii) relies on the fact that
\mu(0)+\mu(1)=1
.
Rearranging terms in (
218
), we are left with

\displaystyle V_{\theta}^{\pi}(0)=\frac{1+\gamma(1-x_{\pi,\theta})\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)}{1-\gamma\big(\mu(1)x_{\pi,\theta}+\mu(0)\big)}=V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)+\frac{1-(1-\gamma)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)}{1-\gamma\mu(0)-\gamma\mu(1)x_{\pi,\theta}}.

(220)

Additionally, the value function of state
1
can be calculated as

\displaystyle V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)

\displaystyle=\pi(0\,|\,1)\left(\frac{1}{2}+\gamma V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)\right)+\pi(1\,|\,1)\gamma\Big[\big((2\gamma-1)+2(1-\gamma)\mu(1)\big)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)+2(1-\gamma)\mu(0)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(0)\Big]

\displaystyle=\pi(0\,|\,1)\left(\frac{1}{2}+\gamma V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)\right)+\pi(1\,|\,1)\gamma\left[\left(1-\frac{2(1-\gamma)}{CS}\right)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)+\frac{2(1-\gamma)}{CS}V_{\theta}^{\pi}(0)\right]

(221)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}\pi(0\,|\,1)\left(\frac{1}{2}+\gamma V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)\right)+\pi(1\,|\,1)\gamma\left[\left(1-\frac{2(1-\gamma)}{CS}\right)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)+\frac{2(1-\gamma)}{CS}\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\leq}\pi(0\,|\,1)\left(\frac{1}{2}+\gamma V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)\right)+\pi(1\,|\,1)\left[\frac{1}{2}+\gamma\left(1-\frac{2(1-\gamma)}{CS}\right)V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}+\gamma V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)-\frac{2\gamma(1-\gamma)}{CS}V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)\pi(1\,|\,1),

(222)

where (i) arises from the elementary property
0\leq V_{\theta}^{\pi}(s)\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
for any
\pi
and
s\in\mathcal{S}
,
and (ii) comes from the assumption (
193
).
The above observation reveals several facts:

•

If we take
\pi(0\,|\,1)=1
, then (
221
) tells us that

V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)=\frac{1}{2}+\gamma V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)=\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)}.

(223)

•

It also follows from (
222
) that for any policy
\pi
, one has

V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)\leq\frac{1}{2}+\gamma V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad V_{\theta}^{\pi}(1)\leq\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)}.

(224)

These two facts taken collectively imply that the optimal policy and the optimal value function obey

\pi^{\star}_{\theta}(0\,|\,1)=1\qquad\text{and}\qquad V^{\star}_{\theta}(1)=\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)}.

(225)

Next, we have learned from (
220
) that

V_{\theta}^{\star}(0)=V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)+\frac{1-(1-\gamma)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)}{1-\gamma\mu(0)-\gamma\mu(1)x_{\pi^{\star}_{\theta},\theta}}.

Note that
1-(1-\gamma)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)\geq 1-(1-\gamma)\frac{1}{1-\gamma}=0
.
Since the function

g(x)=V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)+\frac{1-(1-\gamma)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)}{1-\gamma\mu(0)-\gamma\mu(1)x}

is increasing in
x
and that
x_{\pi,\theta}
(cf. (
219
)) is increasing in
\pi(\theta\,|\,0)
(given that
p\geq q
),
one can easily see that the optimal policy obeys

\displaystyle\pi_{\theta}^{\star}(\theta\,|\,0)=1.

(226)

C.2.4
Proof of auxiliary properties

Proof of claim (
208
).

We begin by proving the property (
208
).
Towards this, let us abuse the notation by considering a MDP trajectory denoted by
\{(s_{t},a_{t})\}_{t\geq 0}
,
and suppose that it starts from
s_{0}\sim\rho^{\mathsf{b}}=\mu
.
It can be straightforwardly calculated that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{s_{1}=0\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{s}\mu(s)\Big\{\pi^{\mathsf{b}}(0\,|\,s)\mathbb{P}\left\{s_{1}=0\mid s_{0}=s,a_{0}=0\right\}+\pi^{\mathsf{b}}(1\,|\,s)\mathbb{P}\left\{s_{1}=0\mid s_{0}=s,a_{0}=1\right\}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\mu(0)\left\{\frac{1}{2}P_{\theta}(0\,|\,0,0)+\frac{1}{2}P_{\theta}(0\,|\,0,1)\right\}+\mu(1)\left\{\frac{1}{2}P_{\theta}(0\,|\,1,0)+\frac{1}{2}P_{\theta}(0\,|\,1,1)\right\}

\displaystyle=\mu(0)\left\{\gamma+(1-\gamma)\mu(0)\right\}+\mu(1)\left\{(1-\gamma)\mu(0)\right\}=\mu(0),

where the last identity holds since
\mu(0)+\mu(1)=1
. Similarly, one can derive
\mathbb{P}\left\{s_{1}=1\right\}=\mu(1)
, thus indicating that
s_{1}\sim\mu
.
Repeating this analysis reveals that
s_{t}\sim\mu
for any
t\geq 0
.
Consequently, one has

d^{\mathsf{b}}(s)=(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\mathbb{P}\big(s_{t}=s\mid s_{0}\sim\rho^{\mathsf{b}};\pi^{\mathsf{b}}\big)\right]=\mu(s),\qquad\forall s\in\mathcal{S}.

Additionally, it it observed that

\displaystyle d^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)=d^{\mathsf{b}}(s)\pi^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)=\mu(s)/2.

(227)

Proof of claim (
210
).

Consider the MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
, whose optimal policy
\pi_{\theta}^{\star}
satisfies
\pi_{\theta}^{\star}(\theta\,|\,0)=1
(see Lemma
11
).
Let us generate a MDP trajectory denoted by
\{(s_{t},a_{t})\}_{t\geq 0}
with
a_{t}\sim\pi_{\theta}^{\star}(\cdot\,|\,s_{t})
,
where we have again abused notation as long as it is clear from the context.
In this case, we can deduce that

\displaystyle d^{\star}(0,\theta)

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\mathbb{P}\big(s_{t}=0\mid s_{0}\sim\rho;\pi^{\star}_{\theta}\big)\pi^{\star}_{\theta}(\theta\,|\,0)\right]=(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\mathbb{P}\big(s_{t}=0\mid s_{0}\sim\rho;\pi^{\star}_{\theta}\big)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\geq}(1-\gamma)\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\rho(0)\big[\mathbb{P}_{\theta}(0\,|\,0,\theta)\big]^{t}\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\geq}(1-\gamma)\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\rho(0)\gamma^{2t}=\frac{1-\gamma}{1-\gamma^{2}}=\frac{1}{1+\gamma}\geq\frac{1}{2},

where in (i) we compute, for each
t
, the probability of a special trajectory with
s_{1}=\cdots=s_{t}=0
and
a_{0}=\cdots=a_{t-1}=\theta
,
and (ii) holds true since
P_{\theta}(0\,|\,0,\theta)\geq p\geq\gamma
.
Taking this together with (
227
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{\min\big\{d^{\star}(0,\theta),\frac{1}{S}\big\}}{d^{\mathsf{b}}(0,\theta)}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{S\mu(0)}=2C,

\displaystyle\frac{\min\big\{d^{\star}(0,1-\theta),\frac{1}{S}\big\}}{d^{\mathsf{b}}(0,1-\theta)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\min\big\{d^{\star}(0,1-\theta),\frac{1}{S}\big\}}{d^{\mathsf{b}}(0,\theta)}\leq\frac{\min\big\{d^{\star}(0,\theta),\frac{1}{S}\big\}}{d^{\mathsf{b}}(0,\theta)}=2C.

(228)

In addition, it is easily seen that
d^{\star}(s,a)=0
for any
s>1
, and that

\frac{\min\big\{d^{\star}(1,a),\frac{1}{S}\big\}}{d^{\mathsf{b}}(1,a)}\leq\frac{1/S}{\mu(1)/2}=\frac{2}{S(1-1/CS)}\leq\frac{4}{S}\leq 2C,

where the first inequality comes from (
227
),
the first identity uses the definition (
192
), and the last two inequalities result from an immediate consequence of (
193
) and
\gamma\geq 1/2
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{CS}\leq\frac{1}{4\gamma}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(229)

As a result, putting the above relations together leads to

\displaystyle C^{\star}_{\mathsf{clipped}}=\max_{(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}}\frac{\min\big\{d^{\star}(s,a),\frac{1}{S}\big\}}{d^{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)}=\frac{\min\big\{d^{\star}(0,\theta),\frac{1}{S}\big\}}{d^{\mathsf{b}}(0,\theta)}=2C.

(230)

Proof of inequality (
211
).

Observing the basic identity (using
\mu(0)+\mu(1)=1
)

\frac{1+\gamma(1-x)\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)}{1-\gamma\big(\mu(1)x+\mu(0)\big)}=V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)+\frac{1-(1-\gamma)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)}{1-\gamma\mu(0)-\gamma\mu(1)x},

we can obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1+\gamma(1-p)\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)}{1-\gamma\big(\mu(1)p+\mu(0)\big)}-\frac{1+\gamma(1-x_{\pi,\theta})\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)}{1-\gamma\big(\mu(1)x_{\pi,\theta}+\mu(0)\big)}=\frac{1-(1-\gamma)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)}{1-\gamma\mu(0)-\gamma\mu(1)p}-\frac{1-(1-\gamma)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)}{1-\gamma\mu(0)-\gamma\mu(1)x_{\pi,\theta}}

\displaystyle=\big(1-(1-\gamma)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)\big)\frac{\gamma\mu(1)(p-x_{\pi,\theta})}{~\underbrace{\left[1-\gamma\big(\mu(1)p+\mu(0)\big)\right]\left[1-\gamma\big(\mu(1)x_{\pi,\theta}+\mu(0)\big)\right]}_{\eqqcolon\,\alpha}~}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma\mu(1)(p-x_{\pi,\theta})}{2\alpha},

(231)

where the last relation arises from the fact (
207
).

The remainder of the proof boils down to controlling
\alpha
.
Making use of the definition of
p
(cf. (
199
)),

\mu(s)
(cf. (
192
)) and
x_{\pi}
(cf. (
206
)),
we can demonstrate that

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=\left[1-\gamma\left(\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)p+\frac{1}{CS}\right)\right]\left[1-\gamma\left(\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)x_{\pi,\theta}+\frac{1}{CS}\right)\right]\leq(1-\gamma p)(1-\gamma x_{\pi,\theta})

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}(1-\gamma p)(1-\gamma q)\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\leq}\Big(1-\gamma\frac{p+q}{2}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma^{2})^{2}=(1-\gamma)^{2}(1+\gamma)^{2}\leq 4(1-\gamma)^{2},

(232)

where (i) holds true owing to the trivial fact that
x_{\pi,\theta}\geq q
for any policy
\pi
(as long as
p\geq q
),
and (ii) is a consequence of the AM-GM inequality.
Substituting it into (
231
) and using the definition (
206
) give

\displaystyle\frac{1+\gamma(1-p)\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)}{1-\gamma\big(\mu(1)p+\mu(0)\big)}-\frac{1+\gamma(1-x_{\pi,\theta})\mu(1)V_{\theta}^{\star}(1)}{1-\gamma\big(\mu(1)x_{\pi,\theta}+\mu(0)\big)}=\frac{\gamma\mu(1)(p-x_{\pi,\theta})}{2\alpha}\geq\frac{\gamma\mu(1)(p-x_{\pi,\theta})}{8(1-\gamma)^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{\gamma\mu(1)}{8(1-\gamma)^{2}}(p-q)\pi(1-\theta\,|\,0)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{3\gamma}{32(1-\gamma)^{2}}\frac{28(1-\gamma)^{2}\varepsilon}{\gamma}\pi(1-\theta\,|\,0)=\frac{21\varepsilon}{8}\big(1-\pi(\theta\,|\,0)\big).

C.3
Proof of Theorem
4

To establish Theorem
4
,
we shall first generate a collection of hard problem instances (including MDPs and the associated batch datasets),
and then conduct sample complexity analyses over these hard instances.

C.3.1
Construction of hard problem instances

Construction of the hard MDPs.

To begin with, for any integer
H\geq 32
, let us consider a set
\Theta\subseteq\{0,1\}^{H}
of
H
-dimensional vectors, which we shall construct shortly.
We then generate a collection of MDPs

\mathsf{MDP}(\Theta)=\left\{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}=\big(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P^{\theta}=\{P^{\theta_{h}}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H},\{r_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H},H\big)\mid\theta=[\theta_{h}]_{1\leq h\leq H}\in\Theta\right\},

(233)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}=\{0,1,\ldots,S-1\},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{A}=\{0,1\}.

To define the transition kernel of these MDPs, we find it convenient to introduce the following state distribution supported on the state subset
\{0,1\}
:

\displaystyle\mu(s)=\frac{1}{CS}\mathds{1}\{s=0\}+\Big(1-\frac{1}{CS}\Big)\mathds{1}\{s=1\},

(234)

where
\mathds{1}(\cdot)
is the indicator function, and
C>0
is some constant that will determine the concentrability coefficient
C^{\star}_{\mathsf{clipped}}
(as we shall detail momentarily). It is assumed that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{CS}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

(235)

With this distribution in mind, we can specify the transition kernel
P^{\theta}=\{P^{\theta_{h}}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H}
of the MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
as follows:

\displaystyle P^{\theta_{h}}_{h}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}p\mathds{1}\{s^{\prime}=0\}+(1-p)\mu(s^{\prime})&\text{if}&(s,a)=(0,\theta_{h})\\
q\mathds{1}\{s^{\prime}=0\}+(1-q)\mu(s^{\prime})&\text{if}&(s,a)=(0,1-\theta_{h})\\
\mathds{1}\{s^{\prime}=1\}&\text{if}&(s,a)=(1,0)\\
\big(1-\frac{2c_{1}}{H}\big)\mathds{1}\{s^{\prime}=1\}+\frac{2c_{1}}{H}\mu(s^{\prime})&\text{if}&(s,a)=(1,1)\\
\big(1-\frac{1}{H}\big)\mathds{1}\{s^{\prime}=s\}+\frac{1}{H}\mu(s^{\prime})&\text{if}&s>1\end{array}\right.

for any
(s,a,s^{\prime},h)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{S}\times[H]
,
where
p
and
q
are set to be

p=1-\frac{c_{1}}{H}+\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{H^{2}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad q=1-\frac{c_{1}}{H}-\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{H^{2}}

(241)

for
c_{1}=1/4
and
c_{2}=4096
such that

\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{H^{2}}\leq\frac{c_{1}}{2H}\leq\frac{1}{8}.

(242)

It is readily seen from the above assumption that

\displaystyle p>q\geq\frac{1}{2}.

(243)

In view of the transition kernel (
C.3.1
),
the MDP will never leave the state subset
\{0,1\}
if its initial state belongs to
\{0,1\}
.
The reward function of all these MDPs is chosen to be

\displaystyle r_{h}(s,a)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}1&\text{if}&s=0\\
\frac{1}{2}&\text{if}&(s,a)=(1,0)\\
0&\text{if}&(s,a)=(1,1)\\
0&\text{if}&s>1\end{array}\right.

for any
(s,a,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times[H]
.

Finally, let us choose the set
\Theta\subseteq\{0,1\}^{H}
.
By virtue of the Gilbert-Varshamov lemma
(
Gilbert, 1952
)
,
one can construct
\Theta\subseteq\{0,1\}^{H}
in a way that

|\Theta|\geq e^{H/8}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\theta-\widetilde{\theta}\|_{1}\geq\frac{H}{8}\quad\text{for any }\theta,\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta\text{ obeying }\theta\neq\widetilde{\theta}.

(248)

In other words, the set
\Theta
we construct contains an exponentially large number of vectors that are sufficiently separated.
This property plays an important role in the ensuing analysis.

Value functions and optimal policies.

Next, we look at the value functions of the constructed MDPs and identify the optimal policies.
For the sake of notational clarity, for the MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
,
we denote by
\pi^{\star,\theta}=\{\pi^{\star,\theta}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H}
the optimal policy,
and let
V_{h}^{\pi,\theta}
(resp.
V_{h}^{\star,\theta}
) indicate the value function of policy
\pi
(resp.
\pi^{\star,\theta}
) at time step
h
. The following lemma collects a couple of useful properties concerning the value functions and optimal policies; the proof can be found in Appendix
C.3.3
.

Lemma 12
.

Consider any
\theta\in\Theta
and any policy
\pi
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle V_{h}^{\pi,\theta}(0)=1+\big(\mu(1)x_{h}^{\pi,\theta}+\mu(0)\big)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)+(1-x_{h}^{\pi,\theta})\mu(1)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)

(249)

for any
h\in[H]
, where

\displaystyle x_{h}^{\pi,\theta}=p\pi_{h}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)+q\pi_{h}(1-\theta_{h}\,|\,0).

(250)

In addition, for any
h\in[H]
, the optimal policies and the optimal value functions obey

\displaystyle\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle V_{h}^{\star,\theta}(0)\geq\tfrac{2}{3}(H+1-h),

(251a)

\displaystyle\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(0\,|\,1)

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle V_{h}^{\star,\theta}(1)=\tfrac{1}{2}(H+1-h),

(251b)

provided that
0<c_{1}\leq 1/2
.

Construction of the batch dataset.

A batch dataset is then generated, which consists of
K

independent
sample trajectories each of length
H
.
The initial state distribution
\rho^{\mathsf{b}}
and the behavior policy
\pi^{\mathsf{b}}=\{\pi^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H}
(according to (
45
)) are chosen as follows:

\displaystyle\rho^{\mathsf{b}}(s)=\mu(s)\quad\text{and }\quad\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)=\frac{1}{2},\qquad\forall(s,a,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times[H],

where
\mu
has been defined in (
234
).
As it turns out, for any MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
, the occupancy distributions of the above batch dataset admit the following simple characterization:

\displaystyle d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)

\displaystyle=\mu(s),\qquad d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s,a)=\frac{1}{2}\mu(s),\qquad\forall(s,a,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times[H].

(252)

Additionally, we shall choose the initial state distribution
\rho
as follows

\displaystyle\rho(s)=\begin{cases}1,\quad&\text{if }s=0,\\
0,&\text{if }s>0.\end{cases}

(253)

With this choice of
\rho
, the single-policy clipped concentrability coefficient
C^{\star}_{\mathsf{clipped}}
and the quantity
C
are intimately connected as follows:

\displaystyle C^{\star}_{\mathsf{clipped}}=2C.

(254)

The proof of the claims (
252
) and (
254
) can be found in Appendix
C.3.4
.

C.3.2
Establishing the minimax lower bound

We are now positioned to establish our sample complexity lower bounds.
Recalling our choice of
\rho
in (
253
),
our proof seeks to control the quantity

\big\langle\rho,V_{1}^{\star,\theta}-V_{1}^{\widehat{\pi},\theta}\big\rangle=V_{1}^{\star,\theta}(0)-V_{1}^{\widehat{\pi},\theta}(0),

where
\widehat{\pi}
is any policy estimator computed based on the batch dataset.

Step 1: converting
\widehat{\pi}
into an estimate
\widehat{\theta}
of
\theta
.

Towards this, we first make the following claim: for an arbitrary policy
\pi
obeying

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\pi_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}\geq\frac{H}{8},

(255)

one has

\displaystyle\big\langle\rho,V_{1}^{\star,\theta}-V_{1}^{\pi,\theta}\big\rangle>\varepsilon.

(256)

We shall postpone the proof of this claim to Appendix
C.3.4
.
Suppose for the moment that there exists a policy estimate
\widehat{\pi}
that achieves

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\big\langle\rho,V_{1}^{\star,\theta}-V_{1}^{\widehat{\pi},\theta}\big\rangle\leq\varepsilon\right\}\geq\frac{3}{4},

(257)
