then in view of (
256
),
we necessarily have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}<H/8\right\}\geq\frac{3}{4}.

(258)

With the above observation in mind, we are motivated to construct the following estimate
\widehat{\theta}
for
\theta\in\Theta
:

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}=\arg\min_{\overline{\theta}\in\Theta}~\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi^{\star,\overline{\theta}}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}.

(259)

If
\sum_{h}\big\|\widehat{\pi}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}<H/8
holds for some
\theta\in\Theta
,
then for any
\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta
with
\widetilde{\theta}\neq\theta
one has

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{h}^{\star,\widetilde{\theta}}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{h}^{\star,\widetilde{\theta}}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}-\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi^{\star,\theta}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}

\displaystyle=2\big\|\theta-\widetilde{\theta}\big\|_{1}-\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi^{\star,\theta}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}

\displaystyle>\frac{H}{4}-\frac{H}{8}=\frac{H}{8},

(260)

where the first inequality holds by the triangle inequality,
the second line arises from the fact
\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)=1
for all
1\leq h\leq H
(see (
251
)), and the last line comes from the properties (
248
) about
\Theta
.
Putting (
259
) and (
260
) together implies that

\widehat{\theta}=\theta
if

\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi^{\star,\theta}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}<\frac{H}{8}<\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{h}^{\star,\widetilde{\theta}}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}

is valid for all
\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta
with
\widetilde{\theta}\neq\theta
.
As a consequence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\widehat{\theta}=\theta\big)\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}<\frac{H}{8}\right)\geq\frac{3}{4}.

(261)

In the sequel, we aim to demonstrate that (
261
) cannot possibly happen without enough samples,
which would in turn contradict (
257
).

Step 2: probability of error in testing multiple hypotheses.

Next, we turn attention to a
|\Theta|
-ary hypothesis testing problem.
For any
\theta\in\Theta
, denote by
\mathbb{P}_{\theta}
the probability distribution when the MDP is
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
.
We will then study the minimax probability of error defined as follows:

p_{\mathrm{e}}\coloneqq\inf_{\psi}\max_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathbb{P}_{\theta}(\psi\neq\theta),

(262)

where the infimum is taken over all possible tests
\psi
(constructed based on the batch dataset available).

Let
\mu^{\mathsf{b},\theta}
(resp.
\mu^{\mathsf{b},\theta_{h}}_{h}(s_{h})
) represent the distribution of a sample trajectory
\{s_{1},a_{1},s_{2},a_{2},\cdots,s_{H},a_{H}\}
(resp. a sample
(a_{h},s_{h+1})
conditional on
s_{h}
) for the MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
. Recalling that the
K
trajectories in the batch dataset are independently generated, one obtains

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{e}}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\geq}1-\frac{K\max_{\theta,\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta,\,\theta\neq\widetilde{\theta}}\mathsf{KL}\big(\mu^{\mathsf{b},\theta}\parallel\mu^{\mathsf{b},\widetilde{\theta}}\big)+\log 2}{\log|\Theta|}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\geq}1-\frac{8K}{H}\max_{\theta,\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta,\,\theta\neq\widetilde{\theta}}\mathsf{KL}\big(\mu^{\mathsf{b},\theta}\parallel\mu^{\mathsf{b},\widetilde{\theta}}\big)-\frac{8\log 2}{H}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{\geq}\frac{1}{2}-\frac{8K}{H}\max_{\theta,\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta,\,\theta\neq\widetilde{\theta}}\mathsf{KL}\big(\mu^{\mathsf{b},\theta}\parallel\mu^{\mathsf{b},\widetilde{\theta}}\big),

(263)

where (i) arises from Fano’s inequality (cf.
(
Tsybakov, 2009
, Corollary 2.6)
) and the additivity property of the KL divergence (cf.
Tsybakov, 2009
),
(ii) holds since
|\Theta|\geq e^{H/8}
(according to our construction (
248
)), and (iii) is valid when
H\geq 16\log 2
.
Recalling that the occupancy state distribution
d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}
is the same for any MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
with
\theta\in\Theta
(see (
252
)), one can invoke the chain rule of the KL divergence
(
Duchi, 2018
, Lemma 5.2.8)
and the Markovian nature of the sample trajectories to obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}\big(\mu^{\mathsf{b},\theta}\parallel\mu^{\mathsf{b},\widetilde{\theta}}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{s_{h}\sim d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}}\left[\mathsf{KL}\big(\mu_{h}^{\mathsf{b},\theta_{h}}(s_{h})\parallel\mu_{h}^{\mathsf{b},\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(s_{h})\big)\right]=\frac{1}{2}\mu(0)\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\mathsf{KL}\left(P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,a)\parallel P_{h}^{\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,a)\right),

where the last identity holds true since (by construction and (
252
))

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{s_{h}\sim d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}}\left[\mathsf{KL}\big(\mu_{h}^{\mathsf{b},\theta_{h}}(s_{h})\parallel\mu_{h}^{\mathsf{b},\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(s_{h})\big)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{s}d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\left\{\sum_{a,s^{\prime}}\pi^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(a\,|\,s)P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)\log\frac{\pi^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(a\,|\,s)P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)}{\pi^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(a\,|\,s)P_{h}^{\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mu(0)\sum_{a}\sum_{s^{\prime}}P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(s^{\prime}\,|\,0,a)\log\frac{P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(s^{\prime}\,|\,0,a)}{P_{h}^{\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(s^{\prime}\,|\,0,a)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mu(0)\sum_{a}\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,a)\parallel P_{h}^{\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,a)\big).

Substitution into (
263
) yields

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{e}}\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{4K\mu(0)}{H}\max_{\theta,\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta,\,\theta\neq\widetilde{\theta}}\,\sum_{h=1}^{H}\left[\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,0)\parallel P_{h}^{\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,0)\big)+\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,1)\parallel P_{h}^{\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,1)\big)\right].

(264)

It then boils down to bounding the KL divergence terms in (
264
).
If
\theta_{h}=\widetilde{\theta}_{h}
, then it is self-evident that

\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,0)\parallel P_{h}^{\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,0)\big)+\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,1)\parallel P_{h}^{\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,1)\big)=0.

(265)

Consider now the case that
\theta_{h}\neq\widetilde{\theta}_{h}
,
and suppose without loss of generality that
\theta_{h}=0
and
\widetilde{\theta}_{h}=1
.
It is seen that

\displaystyle P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(0\,|\,0,0)

\displaystyle=P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0,0)=\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)p+\frac{1}{CS},

\displaystyle P_{h}^{\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(0\,|\,0,0)

\displaystyle=P_{h}^{\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}\big(1-\widetilde{\theta}_{h}\,|\,0,0\big)=\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)q+\frac{1}{CS}.

Given that
p\geq q\geq 1/2
(see (
243
)),
we can apply Lemma
10
to arrive at

\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}\left(P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(0\,|\,0,0)\parallel P_{h}^{\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(0\,|\,0,0)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{KL}\left(\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)p+\frac{1}{CS}\parallel\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)q+\frac{1}{CS}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)^{2}(p-q)^{2}}{\left(\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)p+\frac{1}{CS}\right)\left(1-p-(1-p)\frac{1}{CS}\right)}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}\frac{\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)^{2}(p-q)^{2}}{\left(\left(1-\frac{1}{CS}\right)p\right)\big((1-p)(1-\frac{1}{CS})\big)}=\frac{4(c_{2})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{H^{4}p(1-p)}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{=}\frac{4(c_{2})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{H^{4}\left(1-\frac{c_{1}}{H}+\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{H^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{c_{1}}{H}-\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{H^{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4(c_{2})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{H^{4}\frac{1}{2}\frac{c_{1}}{2H}}=\frac{16(c_{2})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{c_{1}H^{3}},

(266)

where (i) and (ii) make use of the definition (
241
) of
(p,q)
,
and the last line follows as long as
\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{H^{2}}\leq\frac{c_{1}}{2H}\leq\frac{1}{4}
.
Similarly, it can be easily verified that
\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(0\,|\,0,1)\parallel P_{h}^{\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(0\,|\,0,1)\big)

can be upper bounded in the same way.
Substituting (
266
) and (
265
) back into (
264
)
indicates that: if the sample size obeys

\displaystyle N=KH\leq\frac{c_{1}CSH^{4}}{512(c_{2})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}=\frac{c_{1}C^{\star}_{\mathsf{clipped}}SH^{4}}{1024(c_{2})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}},

(267)

then one necessarily has

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{e}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{4K\mu(0)}{H}\max_{\theta,\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta,\,\theta\neq\widetilde{\theta}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\left[\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,0)\parallel P_{h}^{\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,0)\big)+\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{h}^{\theta_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,1)\parallel P_{h}^{\widetilde{\theta}_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,0,1)\big)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{4K\mu(0)}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\frac{32(c_{2})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{c_{1}H^{3}}\geq\frac{1}{4}.

(268)

Step 3: combining the above results.

Suppose that there exists an estimator
\widehat{\pi}
satisfying

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathbb{P}_{\theta}\left\{\big\langle\rho,V_{1}^{\star,\theta}-V_{1}^{\widehat{\pi},\theta}\big\rangle\geq\varepsilon\right\}<\frac{1}{4},

(269)

where
\mathbb{P}_{\theta}
denotes the probability when the MDP is
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
.
Then in view of the analysis in Step 1, we must have

\mathbb{P}_{\theta}\left(\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widehat{\pi}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}<\frac{H}{8}\right)\geq\frac{3}{4},\qquad\text{for all }\theta\in\Theta,

and as a consequence of (
261
), the estimator
\widehat{\theta}
defined in (
259
) must satisfy

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta}\big(\widehat{\theta}\neq\theta\big)<\frac{1}{4},\qquad\text{for all }\theta\in\Theta.

(270)

Nevertheless, this cannot possibly happen under the sample size condition (
267
); otherwise it is contradictory to the result in (
268
). This concludes the proof by inserting
c_{1}=1/4
and
c_{2}=4096
.

C.3.3
Proof of Lemma
12

To start with, for any policy
\pi
, it is observed that the value function of state
s=0
at step
h
is

\displaystyle V_{h}^{\pi,\theta}(0)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{a\sim\pi_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)}\left[1+\sum_{s^{\prime}}P^{\theta_{h}}_{h}(s^{\prime}\,|\,0,a)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(s^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle=1+\pi_{h}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)\left[\big(p+(1-p)\mu(0)\big)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)+(1-p)\mu(1)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\pi(1-\theta_{h}\,|\,0)\left[\big(q+(1-q)\mu(0)\big)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)+(1-q)\mu(1)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)\right]

\displaystyle=1+\Big[p\pi_{h}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)+q\pi(1-\theta_{h}\,|\,0)+\mu(0)-p\pi_{h}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)\mu(0)-q\pi(1-\theta_{h}\,|\,0)\mu(0)\Big]V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mu(1)\Big[1-p\pi_{h}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)-q\pi(1-\theta_{h}\,|\,0)\Big]V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{=}1+\left[x_{h}^{\pi,\theta}+(1-x_{h}^{\pi,\theta})\mu(0)V^{\pi,\theta}_{h+1}(0)+(1-x_{h}^{\pi,\theta})\mu(1)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{=}1+\big(\mu(1)x_{h}^{\pi}+\mu(0)\big)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)+(1-x_{h}^{\pi})\mu(1)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1),

(271)

where (i) is valid due to the choice

\displaystyle x_{h}^{\pi,\theta}=p\pi_{h}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)+q\pi_{h}(1-\theta_{h}\,|\,0),

(272)

and (ii) holds since
\mu(0)+\mu(1)=1
.

Additionally, the value function of state
1
at any step
h
obeys

\displaystyle V_{h}^{\pi,\theta}(1)

\displaystyle=\pi_{h}(0\,|\,1)\left(\frac{1}{2}+V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)\right)+\pi_{h}(1\,|\,1)\left[\left(1-\frac{2c_{1}}{HCS}\right)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)+\frac{2c_{1}}{HCS}V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)\right]

(273)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}\pi_{h}(0\,|\,1)\left(\frac{1}{2}+V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)\right)+\pi_{h}(1\,|\,1)\left[\left(1-\frac{2c_{1}}{HCS}\right)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)+\frac{2c_{1}}{HCS}\left(H-h\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\leq}\pi_{h}(0\,|\,1)\left(\frac{1}{2}+V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)\right)+\pi_{h}(1\,|\,1)\left[\frac{1}{2}+\left(1-\frac{2c_{1}}{HCS}\right)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}+V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)-\frac{2c_{1}}{HCS}\pi_{h}(1\,|\,1)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1),

(274)

where (i) arises from the basic fact
0\leq V_{h}^{\pi,\theta}(s)\leq H-h+1
for any policy
\pi
and all
(s,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times[H]
, and (ii) holds since
\frac{2c_{1}}{HCS}\left(H-h\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}
for
c_{1}
small enough.
The above results lead to several immediate facts.

•

If we choose
\pi
such that
\pi_{h}(0\,|\,1)=1
for all
h\in[H]
, then (
273
) tells us that

\displaystyle V_{h}^{\pi,\theta}(1)=\frac{1}{2}+V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1).

(275)

A recursive application of this relation reveals that

\displaystyle V_{h}^{\pi,\theta}(1)=\frac{1}{2}+V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)=\cdots=\sum_{j=h}^{H}\frac{1}{2}=\frac{1}{2}(H+1-h).

(276)

•

For any policy
\pi
, applying (
274
) recursively tells us that

\displaystyle V_{h}^{\pi,\theta}(1)\leq\frac{1}{2}+V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)\leq\cdots\leq\sum_{j=h}^{H}\frac{1}{2}=\frac{1}{2}(H+1-h).

(277)

The above two facts taken collectively imply that the optimal policy and optimal value function obey

\displaystyle\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(0\,|\,1)=1,\qquad V_{h}^{\star,\theta}(1)=\frac{1}{2}(H+1-h),\qquad\forall h\in[H].

(278)

We then return to state
0
. By taking
\pi
such that
\pi_{h}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)=1
(and hence
x_{h}^{\pi,\theta}=p
) for all
h\in[H]
, one can invoke (
271
) to derive

\displaystyle V_{h}^{\pi,\theta}(0)

\displaystyle=1+\big(\mu(1)p+\mu(0)\big)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)+(1-p)\mu(1)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)

\displaystyle\geq 1+pV_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)\geq\sum_{j=0}^{H-h}p^{j}\geq\sum_{j=0}^{H-h}\Big(1-\frac{c_{1}}{H}\Big)^{j}=\frac{1-\big(1-\frac{c_{1}}{H}\big)^{H-h+1}}{c_{1}/H}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2}{3}(H+1-h).

(279)

To see that why the last inequality holds, it suffices to observe that

\Big(1-\frac{c_{1}}{H}\Big)^{H-h+1}\leq\exp\left(-\frac{c_{1}}{H}(H-h+1)\right)\leq 1-\frac{2c_{1}(H-h+1)}{3H},

as long as
c_{1}\leq 0.5
, which follows due to the elementary inequalities
1-x\leq\exp(-x)
for any
x\geq 0
and
\exp(-x)\leq 1-2x/3
for any
0\leq x\leq 1/2
.
Combine (
279
) with (
278
) to reach

V_{h}^{\star,\theta}(0)\geq V_{h}^{\pi,\theta}(0)\geq\frac{2}{3}(H+1-h)>V_{h}^{\star,\theta}(1).

(280)

Moreover, it follows from (
271
) that

\displaystyle V_{h}^{\star,\theta}(0)

\displaystyle=1+\big(\mu(1)x_{h}^{\pi^{\star,\theta},\theta}+\mu(0)\big)V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(0)+(1-x_{h}^{\pi^{\star,\theta},\theta})\mu(1)V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(1)

\displaystyle=1+\mu(0)V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(0)+\mu(1)V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(1)+\mu(1)\big(V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(0)-V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(1)\big)x_{h}^{\pi^{\star,\theta},\theta}.

(281)

Observing that the function

\displaystyle\mu(1)\big(V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(0)-V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(1)\big)x

(282)

is increasing in
x
(as a result of (
280
)) and that
x_{h}^{\pi,\theta}
is increasing in
\pi_{h}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)
(since
p\geq q
),
we can readily conclude that the optimal policy in state
0
obeys

\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)=1,\qquad\text{for all }h\in[H].

(283)

C.3.4
Proof of auxiliary properties

Throughout this section, we shall suppress the dependency on
\theta
in the notation
d_{h}^{\star}
whenever it is clear from the context.

Proof of claim (
252
).

For any MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
, from the definition of
d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s,a)
in (
46
) and the Markov property, it is clearly seen that

\displaystyle d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h+1}(s)

\displaystyle=d_{h+1}^{\pi^{\mathsf{b}}}(s;\rho^{\mathsf{b}})=\mathbb{P}(s_{h+1}=s\,|\,s_{h}\sim d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h};\pi^{\mathsf{b}}),\qquad\forall(s,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times[H].

(284)

Recalling that
d^{\mathsf{b}}_{1}(s)=\rho^{\mathsf{b}}(s)=\mu(s)
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
, one can then show that

\displaystyle d^{\mathsf{b}}_{2}(0)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\{s_{2}=0\,|\,s_{1}\sim d^{\mathsf{b}}_{1};\pi^{\mathsf{b}}\}

\displaystyle=\mu(0)\left[\pi^{\mathsf{b}}_{1}(\theta_{1}\,|\,0)P_{1}^{\theta_{1}}(0\,|\,0,\theta_{1})+\pi^{\mathsf{b}}_{1}(1-\theta_{1}\,|\,0)P_{1}^{\theta_{1}}(0\,|\,0,1-\theta_{1})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mu(1)\left[\pi^{\mathsf{b}}_{1}(0\,|\,1)P_{1}^{\theta_{1}}(0\,|\,1,0)+\pi^{\mathsf{b}}_{1}(1\,|\,1)P_{1}^{\theta_{1}}(0\,|\,1,1)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu(0)}{2}\left[P_{1}^{\theta_{1}}(0\,|\,0,\theta_{1})+P_{1}^{\theta_{1}}(0\,|\,0,1-\theta_{1})\right]+\frac{\mu(1)}{2}\left[P_{1}^{\theta_{1}}(0\,|\,1,0)+P_{1}^{\theta_{1}}(0\,|\,1,1)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu(0)}{2}\big[(p+q)+(2-p-q)\mu(0)\big]+\frac{\mu(1)}{2}\mu(0)\frac{2c_{1}}{H}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu(0)}{2}\left[2-\frac{2c_{1}}{H}+\frac{2c_{1}}{H}\mu(0)\right]+\frac{\mu(1)}{2}\mu(0)\frac{2c_{1}}{H}=\mu(0),

where the last inequality holds since
\mu(1)+\mu(0)=1
. Similarly, it can be verified that
d^{\mathsf{b}}_{1}(1)=\mu(1)
, thereby implying that
d^{\mathsf{b}}_{2}=\mu
.
Repeating this argument recursively for steps
h=2,\ldots,H
confirms that

\displaystyle d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)

\displaystyle=\mu(s),\qquad\forall(s,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times[H].

(285)

This further allows one to demonstrate that

\displaystyle d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s,a)

\displaystyle=d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(s)\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)=\mu(s)\pi_{h}^{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)=\mu(s)/2,\qquad\forall(s,a,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times[H].

(286)

Proof of claim (
254
).

Consider any MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
, for which we have shown in Lemma
12
that
\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)=1
for all
h\in[H]
.
It is observed that

\displaystyle d_{h}^{\star}(0,\theta_{h})

\displaystyle=d_{h}^{\star}(0)\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)=d_{h}^{\star}(0)=\mathbb{P}\big\{s_{h}=0\,|\,s_{h-1}\sim d_{h-1}^{\star};\pi^{\star,\theta}\big\}

\displaystyle\geq d_{h-1}^{\star}(0)\pi_{h-1}^{\star,\theta}(\theta_{h-1}\,|\,0)P_{h-1}^{\theta_{h-1}}(0\,|\,0,\theta_{h-1})=d_{h-1}^{\star}(0)P_{h-1}^{\theta_{h-1}}(0\,|\,0,\theta_{h-1})

\displaystyle\geq\cdots\geq d_{1}^{\star}(0)\prod_{j=0}^{h-1}P_{j}^{\theta_{j}}(0\,|\,0,\theta_{j})=\rho(0)\prod_{j=0}^{h-1}P_{j}^{\theta_{j}}(0\,|\,0,\theta_{j})

\displaystyle\geq\rho(0)\prod_{j=0}^{h-1}p\geq\Big(1-\frac{c_{1}}{H}\Big)^{H}>\frac{1}{2},

(287)

where the last line makes use of the properties
p\geq 1-c_{1}/H
,
\rho(0)=1
, and

\left(1-\frac{c_{1}}{H}\right)^{H}\geq\Big(1-\frac{1}{2H}\Big)^{H}>\frac{1}{2}

provided that
0<c_{1}<1/2
.
Combining this with (
252
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\max_{h\in[H]}\frac{\min\big\{d_{h}^{\star}(0,\theta_{h}),\frac{1}{S}\big\}}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(0,\theta_{h})}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{S\mu(0)}=2C,

\displaystyle\max_{h\in[H]}\frac{\min\big\{d_{h}^{\star}(0,1-\theta_{h}),\frac{1}{S}\big\}}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(0,1-\theta_{h})}

\displaystyle=\max_{h\in[H]}\frac{\min\big\{d_{h}^{\star}(0)\pi_{h}^{\star}(1-\theta_{h}\,|\,0),\frac{1}{S}\big\}}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(0,1-\theta_{h})}=0,

\displaystyle\max_{a\in\{0,1\},h\in[H]}\frac{\min\big\{d_{h}^{\star}(1,a),\frac{1}{S}\big\}}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(1,a)}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}\frac{1/S}{\mu(1)/2}\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{=}\frac{2}{S\left(1-\frac{1}{SC}\right)}\leq\frac{4}{S}\leq 2C,

where (i) arises from (
252
), (ii) relies on the definition in (
234
), and the final two inequalities come from the assumption in (
235
).
Taking this together with the straightforward condition
d_{h}^{\star}(s)=0
(
s>1
) yields

\displaystyle C^{\star}_{\mathsf{clipped}}=\max_{h\in[H]}\frac{\min\big\{d_{h}^{\star}(0,\theta_{h}),\frac{1}{S}\big\}}{d^{\mathsf{b}}_{h}(0,\theta_{h})}=2C.

(288)

Proof of inequality (
256
).

By virtue of (
250
) and (
251
), we see that
x_{h}^{\pi^{\star,\theta},\theta}=p
for all
h\in[H]
, which combined with (
249
) gives

\displaystyle\big\langle\rho,V_{h}^{\star,\theta}-V_{h}^{\pi,\theta}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=V_{h}^{\star,\theta}(0)-V_{h}^{\pi,\theta}(0)

\displaystyle=\big(\mu(1)p+\mu(0)\big)V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(0)+(1-p)\mu(1)V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(1)

\displaystyle\qquad-\big(\mu(1)x_{h}^{\pi,\theta}+\mu(0)\big)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)-(1-x_{h}^{\pi,\theta})\mu(1)V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\geq}\big(\mu(1)x_{h}^{\pi,\theta}+\mu(0)\big)\left(V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(0)-V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)\right)+\mu(1)\big(p-x_{h}^{\pi,\theta}\big)V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(0)

\displaystyle\qquad+(1-p)\mu(1)V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(1)-\big(1-x_{h}^{\pi,\theta}\big)\mu(1)V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(1)

\displaystyle=\big(\mu(1)x_{h}^{\pi,\theta}+\mu(0)\big)\left(V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(0)-V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)\right)+\big(p-x_{h}^{\pi,\theta}\big)\mu(1)\left(V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(0)-V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(1)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\geq}q\left(V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(0)-V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)\right)+\big(p-x_{h}^{\pi,\theta}\big)\mu(1)\left(V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(0)-V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(1)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(iii)}}{\geq}q\left(V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(0)-V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)\right)+\frac{3}{8}(p-q)\big\|\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(0)-\pi_{h}(0)\big\|_{1}\left(V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(0)-V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(1)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(iv)}}{\geq}q\left(V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(0)-V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)\right)+\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{8H^{2}}(H+1-h)\big\|\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1},

(289)

where (i) holds since
V_{h+1}^{\pi,\theta}(1)\leq V_{h+1}^{\star,\theta}(1)
, (ii) follows from the fact that
x_{h}^{\pi}\geq q
for any
\pi
and
h\in[H]
,
and (iv) arises from the facts (
251
) and the choice (
241
) of
(p,q)
.
To see why (iii) is valid,
it suffices to note that
\mu(1)=1-\frac{1}{CS}\geq\frac{3}{4}
(as a consequence of (
234
) and (
235
)) and

p-x_{h}^{\pi,\theta}=(p-q)\big(1-\pi_{h}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)\big)=\frac{1}{2}(p-q)\big(1-\pi_{h}(\theta_{h}\,|\,0)+\pi_{h}(1-\theta_{h}\,|\,0)\big)=\frac{1}{2}(p-q)\big\|\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}.

To continue, under the condition

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\pi_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}\geq\frac{H}{8},

(290)

applying the relation in (
289
) recursively yields

\displaystyle V_{1}^{\star,\theta}(0)-V_{1}^{\pi,\theta}(0)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{h=1}^{H}q^{h-1}\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{8H^{2}}(H+1-h)\big\|\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}\bigg(1-\frac{c_{1}}{H}-\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{H^{2}}\bigg)^{h-1}\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{8H^{2}}(H+1-h)\big\|\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{>}\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{16H^{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}(H+1-h)\big\|\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{16H^{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}h\big\|\pi_{H+1-h}^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{H+1-h}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\geq}\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{16H^{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{\lfloor H/16\rfloor}2h=\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{8H^{2}}\Big\lfloor\frac{H}{16}\Big\rfloor\Big(\Big\lfloor\frac{H}{16}\Big\rfloor+1\Big).

(291)

Here, (i) follows since

\Big(1-\frac{c_{1}}{H}-\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{H^{2}}\Big)^{h-1}\geq\Big(1-\frac{2c_{1}}{H}\Big)^{H}>\frac{1}{2},\qquad\text{for all }h\in[H]

holds as long as
0<c_{1}\leq 1/4
and
c_{2}\varepsilon/H\leq c_{1}
.
To see why (ii) is valid, we note that
for
any
0\leq x_{1},\cdots,x_{H}\leq x_{\max}

obeying
\sum_{i=1}^{H}x_{i}\geq x_{\text{sum}}
,
the following elementary inequality holds:

\sum_{i=1}^{H}x_{i}a_{i}\geq\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor x_{\text{sum}}/x_{\max}\rfloor}x_{\max}a_{i};

this together with
\big\|\pi_{h}^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{h}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}\leq 2
and (
290
)
reveals that (by taking
a_{h}=h
and
x_{h}=\big\|\pi_{H+1-h}^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{H+1-h}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}
)

\sum_{h=1}^{H}h\big\|\pi_{H+1-h}^{\star,\theta}(\cdot\,|\,0)-\pi_{H+1-h}(\cdot\,|\,0)\big\|_{1}\geq\sum_{h=1}^{\lfloor H/16\rfloor}2h,

thus validating inequality (ii).
As a result, we can continue the derivation to obtain

\eqref{eq:V-diff-intermediate-13579-inf-LB}\geq\frac{c_{2}\varepsilon}{8H^{2}}\frac{\frac{H}{16}\big(\frac{H}{16}+1\big)}{2}>\varepsilon,

(292)

provided that
c_{2}\geq 4096
.
